## SETTLED PREMISES (re-inlined for packet-fed seats)

* xscale fix: in `arm_coproc_i80321_6`, the `crm 0 / crn 0 / l_bit` arm did `r[rd] = i80321_inten;
  fatal("TODO: XScale read from inten?"); exit(1);` — the fix deletes the last two lines (ACCEPT).  The body
  reads `rd` (as an index) and `i80321_inten`; it TESTS neither.  The sibling `crn 4` read is quiet.
* PPC fixes: rlwimi narrows `tmp` to 32 bits and, BEFORE the insertion loop, `if (mb > me) ra = (ra &
  0xffffffff) | ((uint64_t)tmp << 32)`; rldimi rotates a COPY `n = sh` (the old loop consumed `sh`), and
  its mask loop wraps: `m = (m + 1) & 63; } while (m != ((64 - sh) & 63))`.  rldimi snapshots `s = gpr[rs]`
  before rotating it, then `gpr[ra] &= ~tmp; gpr[ra] |= (tmp & s);`.
* Clause (d) DEPENDS-ON enumerates the predicates dominating the edited lines (to the function boundary) and
  operand swaps drawn from fields the guarded body reads; for a PURE DELETION the (d) mutant re-introduces the
  removed behaviour under a DIFFERENT predicate.  A value the body never tests as a case (`idata == k`) is
  clause-(e) DISCLOSURE, not a floor cell.  An UNSHIPPED detector's rows are L1 construction — adding a row
  needs no floor warrant.

## FINDINGS — xscaleinten (panel_20260912_095235_xscalepass2 + the Opus measuring seat)

* **F1** `if (rd == 0) { fatal; exit(1); }` after the load survives all 29 rows.  **Opus MEASURED 29/29** and
  showed it is a live kill (MRC into r0 dies, into r9 survives); codex, grok, glm, deepseek, kimi predicted it.
  Inten reads landed only in r3-r7, r9, r10, r15.  **ACCEPTED.**
* **F2** an exit keyed on an inten bit no row sets survives: the rows drive inten = 0, S1 = 0xA500005A,
  S2 = 0x5AA5F00D, bit 28 (union 0xFFA5F05F; never set: 0x005A0FA0).  minimax, glm, deepseek, kimi, codex, grok;
  **Opus MEASURED bit 22 at 29/29**, live kill.  **ACCEPTED.**
* **CHANGE for F1+F2:** one row, `RD0_ALLONES` — fresh session, r0 poisoned 0xdeadbe00, inten <- 0xffffffff,
  `mrc p6,0,r0,c0,c0,0`, want r0 == 0xffffffff.  One read fires both.  Detector 29 -> 30 rows, gate pin 30.
* **F3** the CONTRACT said "the read loading another register (M5: isteer)"; the measured M5 redirects BOTH
  directions of the crn-0 arm to isteer (`mut_xscale_t2.py`).  codex, grok, kimi, glm.  **ACCEPTED**, header
  corrected; it now also says a read-only redirect was never built (it would red D_S1 by construction).
* **F4** glm: "M5's 27/29 has no consistent mechanism"; deepseek: "INTEN_WRITE_PRESERVES_ISTEER is vacuous under
  M5".  **REJECTED** — both assumed a read-only M5.  Under the two-way M5 the inten write lands in isteer, so the
  row reading isteer gets S1 (`probe_M5.raw`: got 0xa500005a want 0), and inten is never written, so the IRQ
  never enables (ANCHOR_EXC got 0x8024 spin, want 0x18).
* **F5** `if (isteer != 0)` exit survives.  All seats; Opus: EQUIVALENT on this instrument — isteer can only become
  non-zero through a write that itself exits.  **ACCEPTED as disclosure** (NOT COVERED).
* **F6** ACCEPT is the right disposition — codex, grok, kimi, glm, deepseek, minimax.  Qualification (codex, grok):
  the DC21285 precedent my brief cited is a DISPOSITION precedent, not quiet source — `dev_footbridge.c` still has
  its `fatal`+`exit` there.  **ACCEPTED**; my brief overstated it.
* **F7** kimi: D_RESET/D_ZERO cannot vary under a wrong-source mutant whose substitute is also 0; ANCHOR_CONTRAST
  kills M1 by host death, not by its named "spins" channel.  **ACCEPTED as record**, no change (the kills are real;
  the names overstate the discriminator).
* **F8** kimi: NOT COVERED omitted the read-count ceiling (no session makes more than five inten reads) and that
  the ANCHOR rows raise only irq 28.  **ACCEPTED**, two clauses added.

## FINDINGS — ppcrlwimiwidth / ppcrldimimask (panel_20260912_095238_ppcpass2 + Opus)

* **F9** codex: `while (n-- != 0)` -> `while (n-- != 0 && mb != ((64 - sh) & 63))` skips the rotate exactly when
  the mask is full width; every full-width row and census cell used the ALL-ONES source, which no rotate changes.
  **ACCEPTED.** Row `full rot`: SH=24, MB=40 from 0xDEADBEEFCAFEF00D into 0x5A5A.., want 0xefcafef00ddeadbe.
* **F10** glm: rldimi had no rA == rS row (rlwimi has three).  **ACCEPTED as a sibling-parity gap** — but glm's
  literal mutant, `gpr[ra] |= (tmp & cpu->cd.ppc.gpr[rs])`, reads the UNROTATED source and is wrong for every
  SH != 0 whatever the aliasing; I built glm's own "equivalently" form (re-read rS after the clear AND rotate it),
  which differs from the fix only when rA == rS.  Row `alias rs=ra`: r8 = RS_VAL, SH=8, MB=16.
* **F11** Opus: the `census run`/`disasm total` literal pins DO carry a measured kill — its end-test-without-`&63`
  mutant read `census run 0 / 495`, `disasm total 8/16`.  My table-2 note said they had none.  **ACCEPTED**; corrected.
* **F12** grok, kimi, Opus: rotate amount was evidenced only at SH 8 and 40 (all-ones census is rotate-blind).
  **ACCEPTED**: `full rot` adds SH 24; CONTRACT now says an edit keyed on another single SH is not pinned.
* **F13** codex: the rlwimi `wrap fullword` note said 31 low bits; it replaces all 32.  **ACCEPTED**, fixed.
* **F14** codex: both `cr0 record form` rows check CR presence only; deleting `update_cr0` survives.  **ACCEPTED as
  disclosure** — already stated for rldimi; NOT YET stated in the rlwimi header (owed).
* **F15** kimi, minimax, grok: deleting rlwimi's `sh ?` guard is equivalent on this host (`tmp >> 32` at sh=0 is UB
  but evaluates as `>> 0` on x86-64); the line predates the fix.  **ACCEPTED as not-a-gap**, no change.
* **F16** deepseek: a mutant misrotating only at SH 1 or 2 survives.  **ACCEPTED as clause (e)** (an `idata == k`
  shape on SH), now disclosed.

## DISAGREEMENTS

* **D1 — are F1/F2 floor cells or clause-(e) disclosure?**  *Floor:* glm ("the body reads rd and inten"), deepseek,
  minimax, kimi ("one added row closes the two floor-cell classes"), codex for `rd == 0` ("a bounded operand swap:
  an existing equality-to-zero predicate with the body-read index substituted").  *Disclosure:* grok ("the body
  never tests rd; `rd == 0` is the `idata == k` shape"), codex for the inten bit.  **My provisional ruling:** the
  row was added either way (L1, unshipped), so D1 decides only the GRADE in the CHANGELOG and what the rule says
  for the next pure deletion whose body tests nothing.  I lean **disclosure for arbitrary `rd == k` / bit
  predicates, floor for `rd == 0` and "any inten bit" only because one cheap row pins each partition** — and I
  distrust that lean, because it grades by what was cheap.  **Parties: adjudicate your own dispute.**
* **D2 — M5 consistency** (glm, deepseek vs codex, grok, kimi): settled by the mutator text (F4).
* **D3 — literal pins: floor gap (deepseek) or disclosure (grok, kimi, codex)?**  Settled for "carries a kill" by
  Opus's measurement (F11); the floor-vs-disclosure label is still open.

## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

+#  ---- xscaleinten: arm_coproc_i80321_6's read of the modelled inten register (CP6 crm 0 / crn 0)
+#  ended the host with `fatal(); exit(1)' after computing the right value; it is now an ACCEPT.  The
+#  promoted detector is 30 rows: the agy witness's 26, codex's two (D_OP1, INTEN_WRITE_PRESERVES_ISTEER),
+#  D_CRM1_TMR0 (the crm-isolation row), and RD0_ALLONES, added on pass 2 after an exit keyed on
+#  `rd == 0' measured 29/29 against the 29-row instrument.  Kill table 3
+#  (artifacts/xscaleinten/killtable3_20260912/): twelve legs, all on prediction, BASE 30/30, HEAD 12/30;
+#  each pass-2 row the SOLE killer of its own mutant (RD0_ALLONES kills M_RD0 and M_MAGICBIT).  Unreached rows report
+#  BLOCKED and are not passes, so both FAIL and BLOCKED must be zero.  Section shape from the codex
+#  promotion design (codex_promote_20260912b.txt), which said 28; pinned by hand at 30.
+XSCALELOG=$LOGDIR/gate_xscale_inten.log
+xrc=0
+python3 -u xscale_inten_probe.py "$PMAX" gate "$STUB" > "$XSCALELOG" 2>&1 || xrc=$?
+
+#  PARSE BEFORE SCORE: exactly one valid record of each kind.
+if [ "$(grep -c '^XSCALE_RESULT=' "$XSCALELOG")" != "1" ] ||
+   ! grep -Eq '^XSCALE_RESULT=(0|[1-9][0-9]*)/(0|[1-9][0-9]*)$' "$XSCALELOG" ||
+   [ "$(grep -c '^XSCALE_CONTROL=' "$XSCALELOG")" != "1" ] ||
+   ! grep -Eq '^XSCALE_CONTROL=(OK|FAIL)$' "$XSCALELOG" ||
+   [ "$(grep -cx 'XSCALE_PROBE_DONE' "$XSCALELOG")" != "1" ]; then
+    note "xscale probe produced no unique, valid completion record; last lines follow"
+    tail -5 "$XSCALELOG" | sed 's/^/       /'
+    check "xscale probe output parses" "no" "yes"
+else
+    check "xscale probe output parses" "yes" "yes"
+    grep -E ' (ok|FAIL|BLOCKED)$' "$XSCALELOG" | sed 's/^/       /'
+    check "xscale controls" "$(sed -n 's/^XSCALE_CONTROL=//p' "$XSCALELOG")" "OK"
+    check "xscale probe exit status" "$xrc" "0"
+    xres=$(sed -n 's/^XSCALE_RESULT=//p' "$XSCALELOG")
+    #  Pinned by hand to a LITERAL: a pin that follows the probe cannot tell gained rows from lost ones.
+    check "xscale rows run"     "${xres#*/}" "30"
+    check "xscale rows correct" "${xres%/*}" "30"
+    check "xscale rows emitted" "$(grep -cE ' (ok|FAIL|BLOCKED)$' "$XSCALELOG")" "30"
+    check "xscale rows failed"  "$(grep -c ' FAIL$' "$XSCALELOG")" "0"
+    check "xscale rows blocked" "$(grep -c ' BLOCKED$' "$XSCALELOG")" "0"
+fi
+
 gate_end

 	tmp = sh ? ((tmp << sh) | (tmp >> (32-sh))) : tmp;
 
+	/*  A wrapping 64-bit MASK(MB+32, ME+32) selects the DUPLICATED high
+	    word (Book I :578 -- MASK wraps when x > y).  Assignment to a
+	    32-bit MODE_uint_t retains only the low word, which is a sound
+	    representation argument by defined unsigned-conversion semantics
+	    and NOT an accident: the shift is performed at 64-bit width and
+	    the loop below then does the low-word insertion.
+
+	    IT IS NOT A CLAIM THAT ARCHITECTURAL 32-BIT MODE REMOVES HALF THE
+	    REGISTER, and the earlier one-line wording here invited that
+	    reading.  Book I S1.2 (:342) says both execution modes affect all
+	    64 bits when an instruction sets a 64-bit register and the result
+	    is mode-independent, and :1989 describes every GPR as 64 bits --
+	    so a 64-bit processor running in 32-bit MODE still owes this
+	    write.  Whether any such implementation compiles the narrow
+	    variant is NOT established here; if one does, it loses the
+	    architectural high word and that is a defect.  Filed rather than
+	    guessed.  (This file's idiom is MODE32 with #else -- see extsb /
+	    extsh -- and carries no MODE64 conditional, so a future guard
+	    would be #ifndef MODE32.)  */
+	if (mb > me)
+		ra = (ra & 0xffffffffULL) | ((uint64_t)tmp << 32);
+

 		uint64_t mask;

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""ppcrldimimask table-3 mutator for the two pass-2 legs.  usage: mut_rldimi_t3.py <SEC cpu_ppc_instr.c> <out> <LEG>

  D_SKIP_FULL_ROT  the rotate is skipped exactly when the mask is full width (codex, pass 2):
                   `while (n-- != 0)' -> `while (n-- != 0 && mb != ((64 - sh) & 63))'
  D_ALIAS_REREAD   the merge re-reads rS AFTER rA was cleared and rotates that, instead of using the
                   snapshot `s' (glm, pass 2 -- its "equivalently" form).  glm's literal one-line form,
                   `gpr[ra] |= (tmp & cpu->cd.ppc.gpr[rs])', reads the UNROTATED source, so it is wrong
                   for every SH != 0 whatever the aliasing and is not the aliasing mutant (simulated
                   2026-09-12, _scratchpad sim_rldimi_t3.py).  This form differs from the fix ONLY when rA == rS.

Each find-text must occur EXACTLY ONCE or the leg is refused, so a find-text doubles as a presence pin.
"""
import io, sys

LEGS = {
    "D_SKIP_FULL_ROT": ("\twhile (n-- != 0) {\n",
                        "\twhile (n-- != 0 && mb != ((64 - sh) & 63)) {\n"),
    "D_ALIAS_REREAD": ("\tcpu->cd.ppc.gpr[ra] |= (tmp & s);\n",
                       "\t{ uint64_t s2 = cpu->cd.ppc.gpr[rs]; int k = sh;\n"
                       "\t  while (k-- != 0) s2 = (s2 << 1) | (s2 >> 63);\n"
                       "\t  cpu->cd.ppc.gpr[ra] |= (tmp & s2); }\n"),
}

src, dst, leg = sys.argv[1:4]
t = io.open(src, encoding="utf-8").read()
if leg not in LEGS:
    sys.exit("unknown leg %s -- REFUSING" % leg)
old, new = LEGS[leg]
if t.count(old) != 1:
    sys.stderr.write("find-text for %s occurs %d times -- REFUSING\n" % (leg, t.count(old)))
    sys.exit(3)
io.open(dst, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n").write(t.replace(old, new))
print("MUTATED %s" % leg)

Baseline = the CURRENT SEC cpu_arm_coproc.c.  Every leg replaces ONE block that must occur exactly once

       + T*3 + "} else\n" + T*4 + "cpu->cd.arm.i80321_inten = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];\n" + T*3 + "break;")

        head="", write_line="cpu->cd.arm.i80321_inten = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];"):
    return (T*2 + "case 0:" + T + head + "if (l_bit) {\n" + read_extra_pre + T*4 + read_line + "\n" + read_extra_post
            + T*3 + "} else\n" + T*4 + write_line + "\n" + T*3 + "break;")

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
xscale_inten_probe.py -- Regression probe for xscaleinten on -E iq80321.

CONTRACT (vacuity-floor clause e).  Designed to catch, and each MEASURED in kill table 3
(regress/artifacts/xscaleinten/killtable3_20260912/SCORES.txt): the removed fatal()+exit(1)
reinstated verbatim (HEAD); reinstated under a different predicate -- isrc != 0 (M1),
opcode1 != 0 (M_OP1), opcode2 != 0 (M_OPCODE2), rd == 15 (M_RD15), rd == 0 (M_RD0), an inten bit
no other row sets (M_MAGICBIT), or after the fourth read (M_NREAD); the crn-0 arm redirected to
isteer in BOTH directions (M5 -- a read-only redirect was not built; it would red the value rows,
D_S1 first, by construction); the crm=1 tmr0 read returning inten (M_CRM); and an inten write that
also stores isteer (M_ISTEER_WR).
NOT COVERED: the handler ignores opcode1 and opcode2, and the rows pin that for ONE nonzero value
of each, not for every encoding; likewise inten reads land only in r0, r3-r7, r9, r10 and r15, so an
exit keyed on `rd == k' for another k is not pinned (the body indexes by rd and never tests it), nor
is one keyed on an exact inten VALUE (the rows drive 0, S1, S2, bit 28 and all-ones); an exit keyed
on isteer != 0 cannot be pinned by a live session, because the isteer non-zero write itself exits;
no session makes more than five inten reads, so an exit after the sixth is not pinned (M_NREAD
exits after the fourth); the only pending source the ANCHOR rows raise is irq 28;
r15 data and flag semantics beyond survival to the marker
(RD15_SURVIVE); SIB_READ asserts survival only -- neither the destination value nor the absence of
a diagnostic; interrupt-controller behaviour beyond the one enable/contrast path the ANCHOR rows
drive.  The isteer non-zero write, the isrc write and the unknown-tuple exit are host kills this
round RETAINS; they are rowed as controls (SIB_WRITE, S_ISRC_WR, E_UNKNOWN), not repaired sites.
No MMU prologue and no `arm T' are needed: a coprocessor register transfer touches no data memory.
"""

import os, sys, pty, select, time, re, signal

if len(sys.argv) != 4:
    raise SystemExit("usage: xscale_inten_probe.py GXEMUL LEG STUB")
BIN, LEG = sys.argv[1:3]
STUB = os.path.abspath(sys.argv[3])
if not os.path.isfile(STUB):
    raise SystemExit(f"missing load stub: {STUB}")

CODE = 0x00008000
S1 = 0xA500005A
S2 = 0x5AA5F00D
TRR0_SEED = 0x11112222
MARKER = 0x5A
NOP = 0xE1A00000

def hexs(v):
    return "-" if v is None else ("0x%08x" % v if isinstance(v, int) else str(v))

class Session:
    def __init__(self, bin_path, timeout=60):
        self.bin_path = bin_path
        self.pid, self.fd = pty.fork()
        if self.pid == 0:
            os.execvp(bin_path, [bin_path, "-V", "-T", "-E", "iq80321", "-M", "32", "0x%x:%s" % (CODE, STUB)])
            os._exit(127)
        self.buf = ""
        self.dead = False
        self.ready = False
        self.wait_prompt(timeout, mark=0)

    def rd(self, t=0.2):
        r, _, _ = select.select([self.fd], [], [], t)
        if self.fd not in r:
            return False
        try:
            d = os.read(self.fd, 65536)
        except OSError:
            self.dead = True
            return False
        if not d:
            self.dead = True
            return False
        self.buf += d.decode("latin1", "replace")
        return True

    def wait_prompt(self, timeout=30, mark=None):
        if mark is None:
            mark = len(self.buf)
        self.ready = False
        deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout
        while not self.dead:
            remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if remaining <= 0:
                return False
            self.rd(min(0.1, remaining))
            if self.dead:
                return False
            if self.buf[mark:].rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
                self.ready = True
                return True
        return False

    def send(self, cmd_str, timeout=30):
        if self.dead or not self.ready:
            return False
        mark = len(self.buf)
        self.ready = False
        b = (cmd_str + "\n").encode("latin1")
        try:
            os.write(self.fd, b)
        except OSError:
            self.dead = True
            return False
        return self.wait_prompt(timeout, mark=mark)

    def interrupt(self, timeout=2):
        if self.dead:
            return False
        mark = len(self.buf)
        self.ready = False
        try:
            os.write(self.fd, b"\x03")
        except OSError:
            self.dead = True
            return False
        return self.wait_prompt(timeout, mark=mark)

    def get_regs(self):
        regs = {}
        if self.dead:
            return regs
        mark = len(self.buf)
        if self.send("reg", timeout=10):
            seg = self.buf[mark:]
            for m in re.finditer(r"\br([0-9]+)\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)", seg):
                regs[int(m.group(1))] = int(m.group(2), 16)
            slm = re.search(r"\bsl\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)", seg)
            if slm:
                regs[10] = int(slm.group(1), 16)
            fpm = re.search(r"\bfp\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)", seg)
            if fpm:
                regs[11] = int(fpm.group(1), 16)
            ipm = re.search(r"\bip\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)", seg)
            if ipm:
                regs[12] = int(ipm.group(1), 16)
            spm = re.search(r"\bsp\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)", seg)
            if spm:
                regs[13] = int(spm.group(1), 16)
            lrm = re.search(r"\blr\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)", seg)
            if lrm:
                regs[14] = int(lrm.group(1), 16)
                regs["lr"] = int(lrm.group(1), 16)
            pm = re.search(r"\bpc\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)", seg)
            if pm:
                regs["pc"] = int(pm.group(1), 16)
            cpm = re.search(r"\bcpsr\s*=\s*([a-zA-Z]+)", seg)
            if cpm:
                regs["cpsr"] = cpm.group(1)
        return regs

    def close(self):
        dead_before_close = self.dead
        if not self.dead and not self.ready:
            self.interrupt(timeout=2)
        if not self.dead and self.ready:
            self.send("quit", timeout=1)
        try:
            os.close(self.fd)
        except OSError:
            pass
        deadline = time.monotonic() + 2
        st = None
        while st is None:
            try:
                pid, status = os.waitpid(self.pid, os.WNOHANG)
            except ChildProcessError:
                break
            if pid == self.pid:
                st = status
                break
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                try:
                    os.kill(self.pid, signal.SIGKILL)
                except ProcessLookupError:
                    pass
                try:
                    _, st = os.waitpid(self.pid, 0)
                except ChildProcessError:
                    pass
                break
            time.sleep(0.02)
        code = os.WEXITSTATUS(st) if st is not None and os.WIFEXITED(st) else None
        sig = os.WTERMSIG(st) if st is not None and os.WIFSIGNALED(st) else None
        self.dead = dead_before_close
        return code, sig, self.buf

def run():
    print(f"=== XSCALEINTEN PROBE [{LEG}] ===")
    print(f"BINARY: {BIN}")
    rows = []
    
    def report_row(tag, desc, got, want, ok):
        st = "BLOCKED" if ok is None else ("ok" if ok else "FAIL")
        print(f"{tag:<16} {desc:<48} got={hexs(got):<14} want={hexs(want):<14} {st}")
        rows.append((tag, ok, desc, got, want))
        return ok

    # -------------------------------------------------------------
    # ARM D: Value Tracking & Subject Reads
    # -------------------------------------------------------------
    s = Session(BIN)
    for i in range(11):
        s.send(f"r{i}=0xdeadbe{i:02x}")
    s.send(f"r1=0x{S1:08x}")
    s.send("r2=0x00000000")
    s.send(f"r3=0x{S2:08x}")
    s.send(f"r7=0x{TRR0_SEED:08x}")

    prog_d = [
        (0x00, 0xEE047611, "mcr 6,0,r7,cr4,cr1,0"),      # seed trr0
        (0x04, 0xEE148611, "mrc 6,0,r8,cr4,cr1,0"),      # read trr0 (CP6 liveness)
        (0x08, 0xEE109610, "mrc 6,0,r9,cr0,cr0,0"),      # read inten at reset (expect 0)
        (0x0C, 0xEE001610, "mcr 6,0,r1,cr0,cr0,0"),      # inten <- S1
        (0x10, 0xEE104610, "mrc 6,0,r4,cr0,cr0,0"),      # read inten (expect S1)
        (0x14, 0xEE002610, "mcr 6,0,r2,cr0,cr0,0"),      # inten <- 0
        (0x18, 0xEE105610, "mrc 6,0,r5,cr0,cr0,0"),      # read inten (expect 0)
        (0x1C, 0xEE003610, "mcr 6,0,r3,cr0,cr0,0"),      # inten <- S2
        (0x20, 0xEE106610, "mrc 6,0,r6,cr0,cr0,0"),      # read inten (expect S2)
        (0x24, 0xEE10A630, "mrc 6,0,r10,cr0,cr0,1"),     # 5th inten read: opcode2=1 (expect S2)
        (0x28, 0xE3A0005A, "mov r0,#0x5a"),              # continuation marker
        (0x2C, NOP,        "mov r0,r0")                   # landing pad
    ]
    for off, w, _ in prog_d:
        s.send(f"put w 0x{CODE+off:08x}, 0x{w:08x}")
    s.send(f"pc=0x{CODE:08x}")

    dest_map = {0x08: 9, 0x10: 4, 0x18: 5, 0x20: 6, 0x24: 10}
    expect_map = {0x08: 0, 0x10: S1, 0x18: 0, 0x20: S2, 0x24: S2}
    pre_snapshots = {}
    post_snapshots = {}
    d_died_at = None

    for off, w, dis in prog_d[:-1]:
        if off in dest_map:
            regs_pre = s.get_regs()
            pre_snapshots[off] = regs_pre.get(dest_map[off])
        
        ok = s.send("step 1", timeout=10)
        if not ok or s.dead:
            d_died_at = off
            d_stop_reason = "HOST-DIED" if s.dead else "TIMEOUT"
            break
        
        # Snapshot immediately after step while alive
        if off in (0x04, 0x08, 0x10, 0x18, 0x20, 0x24, 0x28):
            post_snapshots[off] = s.get_regs()

    code_d, sig_d, buf_d = s.close()

    # P2 Vacuity Guard Check
    vacuity_violated = False
    for off, rd in dest_map.items():
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""ppcrldimimask -- RUNG-3 DETECTOR for X(rldimi) mask bounds and wrapping defects.

DEFECT.  src/cpus/cpu_ppc_instr.c, X(rldimi):

    uint32_t iw = ic->arg[0];
    int rs = (iw >> 21) & 31, ra = (iw >> 16) & 31;
    int sh = ((iw >> 11) & 31) | ((iw & 2) << 4);
    int mb = ((iw >> 6) & 31) | (iw & 0x20);
    int rc = ic->arg[0] & 1;
    int m;
    uint64_t tmp, s = cpu->cd.ppc.gpr[rs];
    while (sh-- != 0) { ... }
    m = mb; tmp = 0;
    do {
        tmp |= ((uint64_t)1 << (63-m));
        m ++;
    } while (m != 63 - sh);
    cpu->cd.ppc.gpr[ra] &= ~tmp;
    cpu->cd.ppc.gpr[ra] |= (tmp & s);

THE DEFECT HAS TWO DIRECTIONS:
  (1) The rotate loop `while (sh-- != 0)` post-decrements, leaving `sh == -1` on exit
      for every SH (including SH=0).
  (2) The termination test `m != 63 - sh` is therefore evaluated with `sh == -1`,
      becoming `m != 64` always. The mask generated is always MB..63 instead of MB..63-SH.

  Consequences on two classes of encodings:
  - Non-wrapping (MB <= 63-SH): the mask fails to stop at 63-SH and runs all the way
    to 63, OVERWRITING the low SH bits of destination RA that must be preserved.
  - Wrapping (MB > 63-SH): the mask stops at bit 63 and OMITS the required insertion
    into positions 0..63-SH, leaving those destination bits unchanged.

THE FOUR-EDIT REPAIR:
  1. Save SH in a separate variable: `int m, n = sh;`
  2. Consume the copy in the rotate loop: `while (n-- != 0)`
  3. Circular increment in the mask loop: `m = (m + 1) & 63;`
  4. Compare against successor modulo 64: `while (m != ((64 - sh) & 63));`

A partial repair saving SH but using non-wrapping `m++` and `m != 64 - sh` hits
`(uint64_t)1 << -1` at MB=40, SH=40 (undefined behaviour in C).

ARCHITECTURE, PowerPC Book I v2.01:4315-4327 (rldimi RTL and prose):
  n  <- sh[5] || sh[0:4]
  r  <- ROTL64((RS), n)
  b  <- mb[5] || mb[0:4]
  m  <- MASK(b, ~n)               where ~n on 6-bit field is 63-SH
  RA <- (r & m) | ((RA) & ~m)

  MASK(x, y) is defined at Book I:578 as "1s in positions x through y (wrapping if x > y)".
  PEM:6882 and Book I:4031 confirm "It is not possible to specify an all-zero mask."

CLAUSE (e) DISCLOSURE (what this instrument cannot see):
  (1) Model width vs mode width: the probe runs on `-E bareppc -M 64` (PPC970, 64-bit
      model, MSR[SF]=1). It cannot observe a 64-bit CPU operating in 32-bit mode (MSR[SF]=0),
      where `update_cr0` branching on `cpu->cd.ppc.bits == 64` would compare 64 bits instead
      of 32 bits (filed separately as ppccr0modewidth).
  (2) Unimplemented instructions: `rldic` (XO=2), `rldcl` (XO=8), `rldcr` (XO=9) are not
      implemented in the tree's decoder (`goto bad`) and cannot be driven here.
  (3) Full OS boot rigs: no 64-bit PowerPC guest OS boot image exists in the tree; rung 3
      unmodified machine guest execution is the highest achievable rung.

SHIPPED CONTRACT: 29 rows; 495 distinct census cells.  The census uses an all-ones source and
does not test the rotate amount independently of the mask bounds -- the pattern rows do, at
exactly three rotate amounts (SH 8, 24, 40: `pres nowrap', `full rot', `pres wrap'), so an edit
keyed on one other SH value is NOT pinned.  `full rot' is the one full-width mask from a patterned
source; `alias rs=ra' is the one rA == rS row.  The CR row checks readback PRESENCE only; CR0 value
semantics are not covered.  No rlwimi rows here.

Sentinel: PPC_RLDIMI_PROBE_DONE (plus PPC_RLDIMI_RESULT=n/N, PPC_RLDIMI_CONTROL=OK|FAIL)
"""

import os
import re
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile
import time

if len(sys.argv) < 2:
    sys.stderr.write("usage: ppc_rldimi_probe.py <gxemul> [label]\n")
    sys.exit(2)

BIN = sys.argv[1]
LABEL = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "local"
#  The loader needs a 4-byte stub; it is private to this run (the witnesses shared one /tmp path).
_TMPDIR = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="ppc_rldimi_")
DUMMY = os.path.join(_TMPDIR, "dummy.bin")
open(DUMMY, "wb").write(b"\x00\x00\x00\x00")

CODE = 0x10000
M64 = (1 << 64) - 1
SENT = 0x5A5A5A5A5A5A5A5A        # sentinel pre-seeded into destination registers
RS_ONES = M64                    # all-ones source: makes every mask window observable
RS_VAL = 0xDEADBEEFCAFEF00D      # pattern source
SRC = 3                          # rS is always r3; r3 is never a destination
DESTS = list(range(4, 30))       # r4..r29 -- 26 destinations per session

rows = []


def row(name, got, want, note=""):
    assert name not in [n for n, _ in rows], "DUPLICATE ROW NAME %r" % name
    assert len(name) <= 18, name
    ok = (got == want)
    rows.append((name, ok))
    print("%-19s %-20s %-20s %-46s %s" % (name, str(got), str(want), note,
                                          "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
    return ok


# ----------------------------------------------------------------- architecture
def rotl64(x, n):
    n &= 63
    return ((x << n) | (x >> (64 - n))) & M64 if n else x & M64


def mask_b_63(b):
    """MASK(b,63), big-endian bit numbering = the low (64-b) bits."""
    assert 0 <= b <= 63
    return (1 << (64 - b)) - 1


def mask_0_e(e):
    """MASK(0,e), big-endian = the high (e+1) bits."""
    assert 0 <= e <= 63
    return (((1 << (e + 1)) - 1) << (63 - e)) & M64


def mask_range(b, e):
    """MASK(b,e), the architecture's own wrapping mask generator (Book I:578, PEM:6879)."""
    assert 0 <= b <= 63 and 0 <= e <= 63
    m = (mask_b_63(b) & mask_0_e(e)) if b <= e else (mask_b_63(b) | mask_0_e(e))
    assert m != 0, "MASK(%d,%d) came out all-zero, which architecture forbids" % (b, e)
    return m


def want_rldimi(rs_val, ra_val, sh, mb):
    """Architectural rldimi result per Book I:4315."""
    m = mask_range(mb, (63 - sh) & 63)
    return (rotl64(rs_val, sh) & m) | (ra_val & (~m & M64))


def want_rldicl(rs_val, sh, mb):
    return rotl64(rs_val, sh) & mask_b_63(mb)


def want_rldicr(rs_val, sh, me):
    return rotl64(rs_val, sh) & mask_0_e(me)


# -------------------------------------------------------------------- encodings
def enc_md(xo, rs, ra, sh, mbe, rc=0):
    """opcode 30 (rldicl/rldicr/rldimi). Book I format MD."""
    assert 0 <= sh <= 63 and 0 <= mbe <= 63 and 0 <= xo <= 7
    return ((30 << 26) | (rs << 21) | (ra << 16) | ((sh & 31) << 11)
            | ((mbe & 31) << 6) | (((mbe >> 5) & 1) << 5) | (xo << 2)
            | (((sh >> 5) & 1) << 1) | rc) & 0xFFFFFFFF


def RLDICL(ra, rs, sh, mb, rc=0):
    return enc_md(0, rs, ra, sh, mb, rc)


def RLDICR(ra, rs, sh, me, rc=0):
    return enc_md(1, rs, ra, sh, me, rc)


def RLDIMI(ra, rs, sh, mb, rc=0):
    return enc_md(3, rs, ra, sh, mb, rc)


def LI(rd, simm):
    return ((14 << 26) | (rd << 21) | (0 << 16) | (simm & 0xFFFF)) & 0xFFFFFFFF


def ORI(ra, rs, imm):
    return ((24 << 26) | (rs << 21) | (ra << 16) | (imm & 0xFFFF)) & 0xFFFFFFFF


NOP = ORI(0, 0, 0)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------- sessions
def session(setup, insns, timeout=90, machine="bareppc", cpu=None):
    """Place `insns' at CODE, seed registers, step exactly len(insns), dump regs."""
    cmds = list(setup)
    for i, w in enumerate(insns):
        cmds.append("put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (CODE + 4 * i, w))
    if insns:
        cmds.append("unassemble 0x%x 0x%x" % (CODE, CODE + 4 * len(insns)))
    cmds.append("pc=0x%x" % CODE)
    if insns:
        cmds.append("step %d" % len(insns))
    cmds.append("reg")
    argv = [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", machine, "-M", "64"]
    if cpu:
        argv += ["-C", cpu]
    argv += ["0x%x:%s" % (CODE, DUMMY)]
    inp = ("\n".join(cmds + ["quit"]) + "\n").encode("latin-1")
    try:
        p = subprocess.run(argv, input=inp, stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
                           stderr=subprocess.STDOUT, timeout=timeout)
    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as e:
        out = (e.output or b"").decode("latin-1", "replace").replace("\r", "")
        return {}, [], out, "TIMEOUT"
    t = p.stdout.decode("latin-1", "replace").replace("\r", "")
    rc = p.returncode
    how = ("sig%d" % -rc) if rc < 0 else ("exit%d" % rc)
    idx = t.rfind("GXemul> reg")
    regs = {}
    if idx >= 0:
        for m in re.finditer(r"\br(\d\d) = (0x[0-9a-fA-F]+)", t[idx:]):
            regs[int(m.group(1))] = int(m.group(2), 16)
        m_cr = re.search(r"\bcr\s*=\s*(0x[0-9a-fA-F]+)", t[idx:])
        if m_cr:
            regs["cr"] = int(m_cr.group(1), 16)
    dis = []
    di = t.find("GXemul> unassemble")
    if di >= 0:
        seg = t[di:t.find("GXemul> pc=", di) if t.find("GXemul> pc=", di) > 0
                else len(t)]
        for m in re.finditer(r"^[0-9a-f]{16}: ([0-9a-f]{8})\s*(?:<- )?(\S+)"
                             r"(?:\t(\S+))?", seg, re.M):
            dis.append((int(m.group(1), 16), m.group(2), m.group(3) or ""))
    return regs, dis, t, how


def seed(pairs):
    return ["r%d=0x%x" % (r, v) for r, v in pairs]


print("ppcrldimimask RUNG-3 DETECTOR -- binary=%s label=%s" % (BIN, LABEL))
print("machine: -E bareppc -M 64 (UNMODIFIED in-tree; MACHINE_DEFAULT_CPU=PPC970, 64-bit)")
print("wants derived from PowerPC Book I v2.01:4315 (rldimi)")
print("%-19s %-20s %-20s %-46s %s" % ("row", "got", "want", "note", ""))
t0 = time.time()
disasm_checked = 0
disasm_ok = 0

# ============================================================ CONTROL SESSION ==
ctl_insns = [
    LI(9, 0x1234),                       # liveness
    NOP,                                 # seed-holds probe steps over this
    RLDICL(4, SRC, 0, 32),               # clrldi r4,r3,32 -- sibling control
    RLDICR(5, SRC, 0, 31),               # sibling control
]
ctl_setup = seed([(SRC, RS_VAL), (30, SENT)] + [(r, SENT) for r in DESTS])
regs, dis, T, how = session(ctl_setup, ctl_insns)

row("session", how, "exit0", "the control session ran to `quit'")
row("live", hex(regs.get(9, -1)), hex(0x1234),
    "li r9,0x1234 -- guest executes and readback works")
row("seed holds", hex(regs.get(30, -1)), hex(SENT),
    "r30 seeded, never destination: proves write is real")
row("src intact", hex(regs.get(SRC, -1)), hex(RS_VAL),
    "rS is unchanged by instructions")

want_dis = ["li", "nop", "rldicl", "rldicr"]
want_ops = ["r9,4660", "", "r4,r3,0,32", "r5,r3,0,31"]
got_pairs = [(m, o) for _, m, o in dis]
disasm_checked += len(want_dis)
disasm_ok += sum(1 for i, (m, o) in enumerate(got_pairs[:len(want_dis)])
                 if m == want_dis[i] and o == want_ops[i])
row("disasm xcheck", "%d/%d" % (disasm_ok, disasm_checked), "4/4",
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""ppcrlwimiwidth -- RUNG-3 DETECTOR for X(rlwimi) width contamination and high-half insertion defects.

DEFECT.  src/cpus/cpu_ppc_instr.c, X(rlwimi):

    MODE_uint_t tmp = reg(ic->arg[0]), ra = reg(ic->arg[1]);
    uint32_t iword = ic->arg[2];
    int sh = (iword >> 11) & 31;
    int mb = (iword >> 6) & 31;
    int me = (iword >> 1) & 31;
    int rc = iword & 1;

    tmp = sh ? ((tmp << sh) | (tmp >> (32-sh))) : tmp;

    for (;;) {
        uint64_t mask;
        mask = (uint64_t)1 << (31-mb);
        ra &= ~mask;
        ra |= (tmp & mask);
        if (mb == me)


  (1) WIDTH CONTAMINATION:
      In 64-bit compilation, MODE_uint_t is uint64_t. When 1 <= SH <= 31,
      `tmp >> (32-sh)` performs a 64-bit right shift, pulling high-half source
      bits down into positions 0..31 of tmp, contaminating the rotated word.
      Siblings rlwinm and rlwnm cast to uint32_t first; rlwimi failed to do so.
  (2) MISSING HIGH-HALF INSERTION:
      ROTL32 rotates the low word duplicated into both halves (positions 0:31
      and 32:63). The architectural mask is MASK(MB+32, ME+32). When MB > ME,
      the mask wraps from bit 63 to bit 0, covering every bit position in 0..31
      (the entire high half). Therefore, when MB > ME, RA's high 32 bits MUST
      be replaced with the rotated low-word data. The unpatched code left the
      high half of RA untouched.

THE TWO-PART REPAIR:
  1. Narrow tmp on read:
     uint32_t tmp = (uint32_t)reg(ic->arg[0]);
  2. High-half replacement before the walk loop:
     if (mb > me)
         ra = (ra & 0xffffffffULL) | ((uint64_t)tmp << 32);

  *** THE CONDITIONAL MUST PRECEDE THE LOOP. *** The loop walks mb around to me,
  so after it mb == me and the guard would never fire.

ARCHITECTURE, PowerPC Book I v2.01:4310-4327 (rlwimi RTL and prose):
  n  <- SH
  r  <- ROTL32((RS)[32:63], n)
  m  <- MASK(MB+32, ME+32)
  RA <- (r & m) | ((RA) & ~m)

  MASK(x, y) is defined at Book I:578 as "1s in positions x through y (wrapping if x > y)".
  ROTL32 is defined at Book I:4008 / 4126 as rotating bits 32:63 duplicated into both halves.

CLAUSE (e) DISCLOSURE (what this instrument cannot see):
  (1) Model width vs mode width: the probe runs on `-E bareppc -M 64` (PPC970, 64-bit
      model, MSR[SF]=1). It cannot observe a 64-bit CPU operating in 32-bit mode (MSR[SF]=0),
      where `update_cr0` branching on `cpu->cd.ppc.bits == 64` compares 64 bits instead of
      32 bits (filed separately as ppccr0modewidth).
  (2) 32-bit CPU high-half truncation: on 32-bit CPUs (PPC603), registers are 32 bits, so
      high-half insertion is discarded by 32-bit register truncation.
  (3) Full OS boot rigs: no 64-bit PowerPC guest OS boot image exists in the tree; rung 3
      unmodified machine guest execution is the highest achievable rung.

Sentinel: PPC_RLWIMI_PROBE_DONE (plus PPC_RLWIMI_RESULT=n/N, PPC_RLWIMI_CONTROL=OK|FAIL)
"""

import os
import re
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile
import time

if len(sys.argv) < 2:
    sys.stderr.write("usage: ppc_rlwimi_probe.py <gxemul> [label]\n")
    sys.exit(2)

BIN = sys.argv[1]
LABEL = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "local"
#  The loader needs a 4-byte stub; it is private to this run (the witness shared
#  /tmp/ppc_rldimi_dummy.bin with the rldimi witness, so two concurrent runs raced on it).
_TMPDIR = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="ppc_rlwimi_")
DUMMY = os.path.join(_TMPDIR, "dummy.bin")
open(DUMMY, "wb").write(b"\x00\x00\x00\x00")

CODE = 0x10000
M64 = (1 << 64) - 1
SENT = 0x5A5A5A5A5A5A5A5A        # sentinel pre-seeded into destination registers
RS_ONES = M64                    # all-ones source
RS_VAL = 0xDEADBEEFCAFEF00D      # pattern source with differing halves
SRC = 3                          # rS is always r3; r3 is never a destination
DESTS = list(range(4, 30))       # r4..r29 -- 26 destinations per session

rows = []


def row(name, got, want, note=""):
    assert name not in [n for n, _ in rows], "DUPLICATE ROW NAME %r" % name
    assert len(name) <= 18, name
    ok = (got == want)
    rows.append((name, ok))
    print("%-19s %-20s %-20s %-46s %s" % (name, str(got), str(want), note,
                                          "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
    return ok


# ----------------------------------------------------------------- architecture
def rotl64(x, n):
    n &= 63
    return ((x << n) | (x >> (64 - n))) & M64 if n else x & M64


def mask_b_63(b):
    """MASK(b,63), big-endian bit numbering = the low (64-b) bits."""
    assert 0 <= b <= 63
    return (1 << (64 - b)) - 1


def mask_0_e(e):
    """MASK(0,e), big-endian = the high (e+1) bits."""
    assert 0 <= e <= 63
    return (((1 << (e + 1)) - 1) << (63 - e)) & M64


def mask_range(b, e):
    """MASK(b,e), the architecture's own wrapping mask generator (Book I:578, PEM:6879)."""
    assert 0 <= b <= 63 and 0 <= e <= 63
    m = (mask_b_63(b) & mask_0_e(e)) if b <= e else (mask_b_63(b) | mask_0_e(e))
    assert m != 0, "MASK(%d,%d) came out all-zero, which architecture forbids" % (b, e)
    return m


def rotl32(x, n):
    """ROTL[32] per Book I:4008 -- two copies of (RS)[32:63], then rotate."""
    lo = x & 0xFFFFFFFF
    n &= 31
    r32 = ((lo << n) | (lo >> (32 - n))) & 0xFFFFFFFF if n else lo
    return (r32 << 32) | r32


def mask32(mb, me):
    """MASK(MB+32, ME+32) at 64 bits (Book I:4125, 4310)."""
    assert 0 <= mb <= 31 and 0 <= me <= 31
    return mask_range(mb + 32, me + 32)


def want_rlwimi(rs_val, ra_val, sh, mb, me):
    """Architectural rlwimi result per Book I:4310."""
    m = mask32(mb, me)
    return (rotl32(rs_val, sh) & m) | (ra_val & (~m & M64))


def want_rlwinm(rs_val, sh, mb, me):
    """Architectural rlwinm result per Book I:4125."""
    m = mask32(mb, me)
    return rotl32(rs_val, sh) & m


# -------------------------------------------------------------------- encodings
def enc_m(op, rs, ra, sh, mb, me, rc=0):
    """Opcode 20 (rlwimi) / 21 (rlwinm) / 23 (rlwnm). Book I format M."""
    assert 0 <= op <= 63 and 0 <= rs <= 31 and 0 <= ra <= 31
    assert 0 <= sh <= 31 and 0 <= mb <= 31 and 0 <= me <= 31 and 0 <= rc <= 1
    return ((op << 26) | (rs << 21) | (ra << 16) | (sh << 11)
            | (mb << 6) | (me << 1) | rc) & 0xFFFFFFFF


def RLWIMI(ra, rs, sh, mb, me, rc=0):
    return enc_m(20, rs, ra, sh, mb, me, rc)


def RLWINM(ra, rs, sh, mb, me, rc=0):
    return enc_m(21, rs, ra, sh, mb, me, rc)


def LI(rd, simm):
    return ((14 << 26) | (rd << 21) | (0 << 16) | (simm & 0xFFFF)) & 0xFFFFFFFF


def ORI(ra, rs, imm):
    return ((24 << 26) | (rs << 21) | (ra << 16) | (imm & 0xFFFF)) & 0xFFFFFFFF


NOP = ORI(0, 0, 0)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------- sessions
def session(setup, insns, timeout=90, machine="bareppc", cpu=None):
    """Place `insns' at CODE, seed registers, step exactly len(insns), dump regs."""
    cmds = list(setup)
    for i, w in enumerate(insns):
        cmds.append("put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (CODE + 4 * i, w))
    if insns:
        cmds.append("unassemble 0x%x 0x%x" % (CODE, CODE + 4 * len(insns)))
    cmds.append("pc=0x%x" % CODE)
    if insns:
        cmds.append("step %d" % len(insns))
    cmds.append("reg")
    argv = [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", machine, "-M", "64"]
    if cpu:
        argv += ["-C", cpu]
    argv += ["0x%x:%s" % (CODE, DUMMY)]
    inp = ("\n".join(cmds + ["quit"]) + "\n").encode("latin-1")
    try:
        p = subprocess.run(argv, input=inp, stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
                           stderr=subprocess.STDOUT, timeout=timeout)
    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as e:
        out = (e.output or b"").decode("latin-1", "replace").replace("\r", "")
        return {}, [], out, "TIMEOUT"
    t = p.stdout.decode("latin-1", "replace").replace("\r", "")
    rc = p.returncode
    how = ("sig%d" % -rc) if rc < 0 else ("exit%d" % rc)
    idx = t.rfind("GXemul> reg")
    regs = {}
    if idx >= 0:
        for m in re.finditer(r"\br(\d\d) = (0x[0-9a-fA-F]+)", t[idx:]):
            regs[int(m.group(1))] = int(m.group(2), 16)
        m_cr = re.search(r"\bcr\s*=\s*(0x[0-9a-fA-F]+)", t[idx:])
        if m_cr:
            regs["cr"] = int(m_cr.group(1), 16)
    dis = []
    di = t.find("GXemul> unassemble")
    if di >= 0:
        seg = t[di:t.find("GXemul> pc=", di) if t.find("GXemul> pc=", di) > 0
                else len(t)]
        for m in re.finditer(r"^[0-9a-f]{16}: ([0-9a-f]{8})\s*(?:<- )?(\S+)"
                             r"(?:\t(\S+))?", seg, re.M):
            dis.append((int(m.group(1), 16), m.group(2), m.group(3) or ""))
    return regs, dis, t, how

## THE VACUITY FLOOR AND THE DE-ESCALATION CLAUSE (flagship adjudication, 2026-08-21)

**"Weak earns its own round; vacuous does not get to ship" IS A RATIONALISATION UNTIL A FLOOR IS
ATTACHED** �?" the adjudicating seat said so about the main loop's own call, and it was right:
*weak* has no lower bound, so any detector that kills one mutant can claim it.

> **THE FLOOR.** A detector is VACUOUS �?" replace it IN-ROUND �?" unless it (a) fails on the
> pre-fix build, (b) fails on a **VERBATIM REINSTATEMENT of the removed defect at EVERY fixed
> site**, and (c) fails on a null medium (a stale copy, a comment-only file �?" i.e. it carries
> identity rows). Below the floor, the round's own claim to have "shipped a detector with the
> fix" is a WRONG RECORD, which the stopping rule already fixes in-round.
>
> ### *** CLAUSE (d) �?" NULL-REPAIR RED. Adopted 2026-08-23 by OWNER RULING, on the round
> that measured its case. ***
>
> **(d) The detector must also fail when the fix is PRESENT but its REPAIR IS NULLED �?"
> the guard kept, its effect removed.**
>
> **(b) DOES NOT IMPLY (d), and #461 measured both in the same hour on the same detector:**
>
> | mutant | shape | score |
> |---|---|---|
> | `m_dmarevert` | (b): reinstate the `exit(1)` verbatim | **22/31 �?" killed** |
> | `m_align1` | (d): keep the guard, weaken `& 3` to `& 1` | **27/27 �?" ESCAPED** |
>
> The detector passed (a), (b) and (c) and was still blind to a ONE-CHARACTER edit that
> re-opened the very defect the round had just closed. (b) restores the DEFECT; (d) neuters
> the FIX. A detector can be sensitive to one and blind to the other, because they fail at
> different sites: (b) fails where the old code was, (d) fails where the new code is.
>
> **HOW TO BUILD THE (d) MUTANT �?" and it is the same discriminator the accept-row rule
> already uses.** Take each predicate the fix **DEPENDS ON** and weaken it to the
> least-restrictive form that still *reads* like the same check: a mask narrowed
> (`& 3` -> `& 1`), a condition qualified (`if (X)` -> `if (X && writeflag == MEM_WRITE)`),
> a bound relaxed. **Not deletion** �?" deletion is (b) wearing (d)'s clothes and is usually
> already covered. The test is whether a plausible future edit could produce it.
>
> **WHY THE ONE-CHARACTER FORM IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS.** `dma alias`, the row written
> specifically to pin that repair, was **STRUCTURALLY BLIND** to it: it used an ODD address,
> which both guard forms drop. *The row aimed at the defect could not see the defect return.*
> A row written from the FIX's intent tests the intent, not the boundary �?" so (d)'s mutant
> has to come from an adversary, which is exactly what the ask-again buys.
>
> **COST: one mutant per repair predicate**, the same order as the accept-row rule (#448 = 4,
> #449 = 1, #461 = 4 across two arms). It is bounded by the diff, so it cannot become #392:
> the predicates are the ones the fix ADDED, and that list is finite and visible.
>
> ### *** "THE PREDICATE THE FIX INTRODUCED" ENUMERATED THE EMPTY SET FOR MOST OF THIS
> ### QUEUE, AND NOBODY NOTICED FOR THREE ROUNDS. Corrected 2026-08-24 -- clause (d) now
> ### reads DEPENDS ON, not INTRODUCED. ***
>
> Clause (d) says *keep the guard, null its repair*. As written it enumerated only
> predicates the diff ADDS -- and **the two commonest fix shapes here add none**:
>
> * a **pure deletion** introduces no predicate at all;
> * a **DE-FATALISATION** introduces none either -- it changes what an EXISTING
>   predicate's branch does. That is most of this project's queue.
>
> So for those rounds clause (d) enumerated **nothing**, and a round could satisfy the
> floor while having built no (d) mutant whatever. `#461` measured (d) successfully only
> because its fix happened to ADD a predicate (an alignment mask) for the mutant to weaken.
>
> **Reading it as DEPENDS ON fixes both cases at once**, and it is why this now replaces a
> narrower deletion-only rule written earlier the same day: that rule was a special case of
> this gap, and two overlapping statements of one rule is the accumulation this carrier is
> supposed to refuse.
>
> **The two shapes, once the enumeration is right:**
> * **fix ADDS a predicate** -> weaken the added one (unchanged; the original rule).
> * **fix adds none** -> the (d) mutant reroutes an EXISTING guard's repair. For a
>   de-fatalisation: **swap the guard's operand for a field the guarded BODY reads.**
>   For a pure deletion: **re-introduce the removed behaviour under a DIFFERENT predicate**
>   -- not the same one widened, which is (b) wearing (d)'s clothes.
>
> *** THE MEASURED CASE, and it is why this matters rather than being tidy: ***
> `#462` de-fatalised two arms. Its guard reads `d->data`; the body it guards reads
> `d->data` AND `d->data_len`; the two are set together in one place and cleared in two
> places that touch only ONE of them. Swapping the guard's operand to `d->data_len == 0`
> **scored a CLEAN SWEEP on a 52-row detector that had just passed twelve mutants**, and
> restored a NULL-pointer read of 408 bytes. Under the old wording that was coverage debt;
> under DEPENDS ON it is a **floor breach, L2, fix in-round** -- which is what the round
> did, before the wording was corrected.
>
> #### *** THE BOUND, and it is not optional: "DEPENDS ON" INVITES TRANSITIVE DATA-FLOW
> #### CHASING, which is unbounded and is how this becomes `#392`. Named 2026-08-24 by the
> #### adjudicating seat, in the same breath as upholding the correction. ***
>
> > **DEPENDS ON enumerates exactly two things: (1) the predicates DOMINATING the diff's
> > edited lines, walked up only to the ENCLOSING FUNCTION BOUNDARY -- never into callees
> > or callers; and (2) for operand-swap forms, swaps drawn ONLY from fields the guarded
> > BODY reads (the guard-field/body-field grep the pre-table check already mandates).**
>
> That is finite, derivable from the diff by reading, and **it provably contains the
> measured case**: the `d->data == NULL` guard dominates the edited decline (clause 1), and
> `data_len` is a body-read field (clause 2), so `M11` falls out mechanically rather than
> by inspiration. It cannot become `#392` because the enumeration stays closed over the
> diff's own hunks -- the same closure the INTRODUCED wording had, without the empty-set
> defect that made INTRODUCED enumerate nothing for a de-fatalisation.
>
> #### *** JOINT CELLS -- adjudicated 2026-09-04 on `pcc2widthkills` pass 10 by the flagship seat, after
> #### a measuring seat's CONJUNCTION of two body-read counts escaped 190/190 AND THE SAME SEAT ARGUED IT
> #### WAS NOT A BREACH.  Both proposed bounds were rejected; the one adopted came from neither. ***
>
> > **DEPENDS-ON enumerates JOINT cells over the guard's operands and the body-read lvalues EXACTLY
> > WHEN THE GUARDED BODY'S OWN CODE DISTINGUISHES THEM.**  For each lvalue take the PARTITION the
> > body's own tests induce (`pending > 0` gives {0, >0}; `relative_addr == PCCTWO_T1ICR` gives
> > {T1, T2}); the enumerated cells are the CROSS-PRODUCT of those partitions -- never arbitrary
> > boolean functions of the read-set.  A mutant exempting a single cell whose reachability is
> > MEASURED is a floor breach; close the whole cross-product in ONE diff and write into the
> > contract which cells are rowed.  A value or combination the body never tests as a case
> > (`idata == k`; a byte no conditional distinguishes; both counts >= 2 where the body tests `> 0`)
> > is DISCLOSED under clause (e).
>
> **Why this form and not the two that were argued.**  The dissent's discriminator -- *a plausible
> future edit* -- failed three ways: plausibility is not derivable by reading; the mutant was written
> and measured, so it is an edit someone writes; and "single-operand is complete" is measurably
> false, since `&& idata != 2` is single-operand and escapes.  The main loop's own bound -- *pairs of
> body-read lvalues over a finite reachable space* -- was rejected as SELF-REFUTING: the counts' raw
> value space is 2^32 apiece, so "finite" does no bounding work on its motivating case (a C `int` is
> finite -- the first wording here said it was not; codex and glm caught it on pass 11); "reachable
> guest state" would force rowing
> `idata == 2` that the same record discloses; and a PAIRS cap misses `#499`'s four axes.  The
> body-tested-partition form is finite (a product of small partition counts), derivable from the
> diff by reading the body's own `if`s, and it enumerates `#499`'s width x component x direction x
> position cells from `len`, `writeflag` and `relative_addr` -- which neither argued bound reached.
>
> **The measured case.**  The TxICR body tests both pending counts at `> 0` and the label at
> `== T1ICR` / `T2ICR`, so the closure is {T2, T1} x {p1 = 0, > 0} x {p2 = 0, > 0} -- eight cells.
> sets INT before any guard runs), which is the honest form of a cell nobody rows.
>
> *Two records lessons from the same pass.  A CORRECTION IS NOT SAFER THAN THE THING IT CORRECTS: pass 10
> accepted "the store mask is a writer of ICLR" and made a true sentence false -- `0x17 & 0x08 == 0`, one
> AND that nobody ran.  And a PREDICTION COPIED INTO A MEASURED COLUMN is the frozen-number class in a new
> coat: a table said (0x13) where the log said 0x33; the score matched by coincidence and the value was a
> wrong record until a seat read the log.*
>
> *Count the arguments, not the seats: seven of eight landed seats put the pair inside the closure,
> and the one that did not (minimax) was reading the CONTRACT's per-lvalue wording rather than the
> clause -- the wording the discriminator sentence now replaces.*

> ### *** THE PRE-TABLE CHECK -- three greps, ~30 min, and it protects the ASK-AGAIN'S
> ### CAP rather than the clock. Adopted 2026-08-24; two seats derived it independently. ***
>
> > ## *** MANDATORY as of 2026-08-28, by flagship adjudication. It was RECOMMENDED prose for
> > ## four days and TWO rounds skipped it -- which is this file's own structure-first trigger,
> > ## met exactly. ***
> >
> > **SCOPE, and this is what answers the cost objection: it binds EXACTLY the population the
> > vacuity floor already binds** -- any round shipping or EXTENDING a kill-table-backed
> > detector. **Not every round.** Records-only, documented-only and table-less harness rounds
> > are untouched.
> >
> > **FORM: recorded OUTPUT, never assertion** -- the two-part-clearance precedent, *run it, do
> > not assert it*. One `PRETABLE.out` in the round's kill-table artifacts dir (or a probe-header
> > section), **written BEFORE table 1 runs so its own mtime proves the order** -- the same
> > order discriminator the oracle-correction rule uses. It carries: the sibling arms compared
> > and any asymmetry; each enumerated claim sentence mapped to a row or an owed sentence; the
> > guard-field/body-field lists and any disagreeing-state row owed; and per row, the mutant it
> > kills and the observable that differs.
> >
> > **Any check may discharge `N/A` WITH ITS REASON RECORDED. An unrun check and an inapplicable
> > one must not render the same** -- this project's oldest rule about blanks.
> >
> > ### *** AND A THIRD FAILURE MODE, MEASURED 2026-08-28: THE BLOCK WAS RUN, ITS ROWS WERE
> > ### PRESCRIBED, AND THEY WERE LOST WHEN THE ROW LIST WAS REWRITTEN THREE HOURS LATER. ***
> >
> > `#490`'s `PRETABLE.out` named two mutants at **16:05** -- a latch-bit collision and a brace
> > slip -- and prescribed a row for each (`crossbit`, `latch cond`). At **18:45** a nine-seat
> > panel found a DIFFERENT defect (the row set was site-asymmetric), the addition list was
> > rewritten to carry the panel's findings, and **both earlier rows silently vanished.** The
> > measuring seat then BUILT both mutants and both escaped the committed 13-row detector --
> > two clause-(d) floor breaches that the round's own artifact had predicted and specified.
> >
> > **Nothing was forgotten in the sense of never known.** The knowledge sat in the file that
> > exists precisely to be consulted at row-writing time, and did not survive a rewrite in a
> > DIFFERENT artifact. The new list was not careless -- it correctly added everything the
> > panel had just found.
> >
> > > **A ROW LIST REWRITTEN FROM A PANEL'S FINDINGS IS DIFFED AGAINST THE PRE-TABLE BLOCK'S
> > what caught every one of them -- and the exemplar's count is what was wrong.**  A wrong exemplar in
> > the paragraph that mints a check gets reused as the check's warrant, which is why it is corrected
> > here rather than left to the CHANGELOG (`#489`'s block carries the same correction).
>
> **THE COST WAS NOT MISSING KNOWLEDGE.  IT WAS KNOWLEDGE LIVING WHERE NOBODY CONSULTS IT AT
> ROW-WRITING TIME** -- this file's trigger-versus-memory finding, again.
>
> #### AND THE OBVIOUS GENERALISATION IS UNBOUNDED, SO IT IS NOT THE ONE ADOPTED
>
> The tempting sentence is *"every VALUE and every STATE in which a guard refuses needs a
> decline-channel row."*  **That licenses unbounded row growth -- the `#392` shape** -- and the
> adjudication that produced this line rejected it for exactly that reason. The distinction:
>
> * **ROW DEMAND** -- *which* refusals need pinning -- is ALREADY bounded and is not touched:
>   the floor clauses, the (d) DEPENDS-ON closure, one accept row per conditioned predicate,
>   one anchor row per position claim, one ask-again. **Rows are owed PER ENUMERATED MUTANT,
>   never per refusing state**; two states no enumerated mutant distinguishes collapse into one
>   row. That enumeration is diff-closed and finite.
> * **ROW VALIDITY** -- what `#489` actually measured -- is the CHANNEL line above, and it is a
>   WRITE-TIME check on rows that already exist.
>
> > **A FILTER CAN REJECT OR REPAIR A ROW; IT CAN NEVER DEMAND A NEW ONE -- SO IT CANNOT GROW
> > WITHOUT BOUND.**  That is the stop condition, and it is structural rather than a cap: the
> > generator side is untouched.
>
> *Mechanising it -- a `# kills: <mutant> via <channel>` comment per row -- is FILED, not
> > warrant RE-RUNS THE DEATH MEASUREMENT.**
>
> Naming the warrant is what made the record falsifiable �?" it is the only reason the
> claim could be re-tested at all. *"Deleted, absence recorded as the result" was the
> right disposition; the missing half was the expiry check.*
>
> #### MULTI-PART MUTANTS ARE LICENSED, NOT THE DEFAULT
>
> The main loop proposed making multi-part the default form. **The adjudicating seat
> said no**, and gave the boundary: the default stays the SMALLEST SINGLE EDIT, with
> multi-part licensed in exactly two shapes:
>
> * **(i) verbatim reinstatement of what shipped** �?" floor clause (b) applied to a
>   harness tool, taking however many edits the historical diff took;
> * **(ii) reach-the-guard composites where EVERY added edit is first measured GREEN
>   ALONE** �?" e.g. a guard unreachable under the shipped regex, where the other edits
>   are the only road to it.
>
> **That second condition is what answers the unfalsifiability objection**, which the
> main loop had raised against its own proposal: an unconstrained composite can be
> steered until it fails for an unrelated reason, while a composite whose parts are
> proven inert can only fail through the interaction being claimed.
>
> Bonus property worth knowing: a driver that treats MUTANT-TEXT-NOT-FOUND as a FAIL
> turns every find-text into a **presence pin**, so a multi-part mutant additionally
> pins the existence of each feature it edits.

> ### THE FOUR REASONS THAT LICENSE AN IN-ROUND FIX OF DETECTOR MATERIAL
>
> Everything else is FILED. Named so no round has to re-argue it:
>
> * **L1 �?" pre-ship construction.** The row is part of the deliverable still being built. A row
>   measured vacuous before commit is unfinished, not a reopening. **The stopping rule governs
>   findings against SHIPPED material only.**
> * **L2 �?" floor breach.** The shipped detector fails vacuity-floor clause (a), (b) or (c) �?"
>   replace in-round however late it is found.
> * **L3 �?" undischarged obligation.** The row is the sole carrier of something the round OWES
>   (a de-escalation accept row; the "shipped a detector" claim itself). A vacuous sole carrier
>   makes the round's record false.
> * **L4 �?" wrong record.** Prose or comments claiming coverage a measurement refutes.

This resolves an apparent inconsistency: #447's escapes were FILED while #448's and #449's were
fixed in-round. #447's were measured against a **shipped** detector sitting above the floor �?"
coverage debt. #448's and #449's were caught **inside their own construction** (L1), and #448's
`return`�+'`break` variant additionally re-reached the removed `exit(1)`, which is L2.

**A disjointness assertion over the latch classes was REJECTED, on three measured grounds** �?" the
class bit-domains overlap pervasively today, so the general form is unsatisfiable; separation
rests on the array INDICES rather than on the constants, so the assert would guard the wrong
invariant; and for the one disjoint pair the values are hardware register layouts that cannot
legitimately change, so it could never fire. **The real hazard is a duplicate class ID**, and its
zero-cost structural fix is converting the `SH4_VAL_*` defines to a C99 enum ending in
`_NCLASS`, where duplicates are impossible by construction.

each finding defects in the previous pass's fixes, and the chain did not converge.
> A review finding gets FIXED IN THIS ROUND only if it is a MEASURED FALSE PASS or a WRONG
> RECORD. Everything else gets FILED.
That rule alone would have ended #392 at pass-2c instead of 2f.

> ### *** THE REPAIR CHAIN -- a repair made in response to a pass 2 OWES ITS OWN PASS 2.
> ### Adjudicated 2026-08-31, then CORRECTED TWICE by the adjudicating seat in the same day,
> ### and both corrections came from the chain that minted it. ***
>
> **THE OBLIGATION FOLLOWS THE MATERIAL, NOT THE NARRATIVE.** A round that repairs a detector
> in response to a panel has produced NEW floor-bound material, and "the round already had its
> pass 2" is a fact about the narrative. `#499` ran four passes and **the first three each
> found a real defect, two of them MEASURED CLEAN SWEEPS of a detector that had just come back
> green.**
>
> **THE STOP CONDITION: one pass per floor-bound diff, closing on the FIRST PASS THAT FINDS
> ZERO BREACHES** -- subject to the two corrections below, without which it closes early on a
> live escape.
>
> #### CORRECTION 1 -- A ZERO NEEDS A VALIDITY CONDITION ON THE PASS, AND A DENOMINATOR
>
> As first written, "zero breaches" counted a pass's OUTPUT with no condition on the pass
> itself. **Three briefs that day went out with the diff missing** -- a `git show | head -N`
> inlined the commit message instead -- and a panel answering "no breaches" against an empty
> packet would have CLOSED THE CHAIN.
>
> > **A closing pass must be a VALID pass: the diff actually delivered; the roster complete or
> > its misses named per the seat ledger; and the zero carried WITH ITS DENOMINATOR -- which
> > cells, which mutant shapes, were swept.** *"Zero found"* and *"zero looked for"* must not
> > render the same, which is this project's oldest rule about blanks turned on its own stop
> > condition.
>
> That also converts the rule's finishing incentive into a checkable artifact: **a bare zero
> does not close; a zero with an enumeration does.**
>
> #### CORRECTION 2 -- SCOPE: EFFORT ON THE DIFF, THE FLOOR QUESTION ON THE COMPOSITE
>
> *"Scoped to that diff"* is ambiguous and **the dangerous reading would have closed `#499`
> over a live total escape.** The breach that pass 3 found was not in that pass's two hunks --
> the hunks were fine. It lived in the SHIPPED COMPOSITE detector's coverage of the ORIGINAL
> fix's mutant space.
>
> > **Review EFFORT is scoped to the repair diff, so settled design is not re-litigated. The
> > FLOOR QUESTION -- does a clean-sweep mutant exist in the (d) enumeration -- is asked of the
> > SHIPPED COMPOSITE on every pass.**
>
> #### THE BOUND IS IMPORTED, NOT INTRINSIC -- state the dependency or the rule overclaims
>
> The stop condition does not bound the chain and must not pretend to. The bound comes from
> **the (d) DEPENDS-ON enumeration being diff-closed and finite**, and from *zero breaches*
> meaning **zero SURVIVING ADJUDICATION** rather than zero raised -- otherwise one stubborn
> seat blocks closure forever, against the count-arguments-not-seats rule. With those imports
> the chain is infinite only if every repair is defective, in which case every pass is buying a
> real defect off the ship.
>
> #### *** THE AMENDMENT THAT ACTUALLY ENDS CHAINS, and it is the part worth carrying. ***
>
> `#499`'s three breaches were **THE SAME CLASS THREE TIMES** -- a cell of one state space left
> empty, repaired at exactly the address the mutant had used, leaving the symmetric cell open
> for the next pass. Pass 1 found *width* unpinned; pass 2 found *component*; pass 3 found
> *direction* AND *position*. **Pass-per-instance converges LINEARLY IN CELLS**, and 28 -> 34

/*
 *  arm_coproc_i80321_6():
 *
 *  Intel 80321 coprocessor 6.
 */
void arm_coproc_i80321_6(struct cpu *cpu, int opcode1, int opcode2, int l_bit,
	int crn, int crm, int rd)
{
	switch (crm) {

	case 0:	switch (crn) {
		case 0:	if (l_bit) {
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.i80321_inten;
			} else
				cpu->cd.arm.i80321_inten = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			break;
		case 4:	if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.i80321_isteer;
			else {
				cpu->cd.arm.i80321_isteer = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
				if (cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] != 0) {
					fatal("ARM xscale interrupt steering"
					    " is not yet implemented\n");
					exit(1);
				}
			}
			break;
		case 8:	if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.i80321_isrc;
			else {
				cpu->cd.arm.i80321_isrc = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
				fatal("TODO: XScale int ack?\n");
				exit(1);
			}
			break;
		default:goto unknown;
		}
		break;

	case 1:	/*  fatal("TIMER opcode1=%i opcode2=%i crn="
    "%i crm=%i rd=%i l=%i)\n", opcode1, opcode2, crn, crm, rd, l_bit);  */

		switch (crn) {
		case 0:	/*  tmr0:  */
			if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.tmr0;
			else
				cpu->cd.arm.tmr0 = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			break;
		case 1:	/*  tmr1:  */
			if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.tmr1;
			else
				cpu->cd.arm.tmr1 = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			break;
		case 2:	/*  tcr0:  */
			if (l_bit) {
				/*  NOTE/TODO: Ugly hack: timer increment  */
				cpu->cd.arm.tcr0 ++;
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.tcr0;
			} else {
				cpu->cd.arm.tcr0 = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			}
			break;
		case 3:	/*  tcr1:  */
			if (l_bit) {
				/*  NOTE/TODO: Ugly hack: timer increment  */
				cpu->cd.arm.tcr1 ++;
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.tcr1;
			} else {
				cpu->cd.arm.tcr1 = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			}
			break;
		case 4:	/*  trr0:  */
			if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.trr0;
			else
				cpu->cd.arm.trr0 = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			break;
		case 5:	/*  trr1:  */
			if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.trr1;
			else
				cpu->cd.arm.trr1 = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			break;
		case 6:	/*  tisr:  */
			if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.tisr;
			else {
				/*  Writing clears interrupts:  */
				cpu->cd.arm.tisr &= ~cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];

				if (!(cpu->cd.arm.tisr & TISR_TMR0))
					INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(
					    cpu->cd.arm.tmr0_irq);
				if (!(cpu->cd.arm.tisr & TISR_TMR1))
					INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(
					    cpu->cd.arm.tmr1_irq);
			}
			break;
		case 7:	/*  wdtcr:  */
			if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.wdtcr;
			else
				cpu->cd.arm.wdtcr = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			break;
		default:goto unknown;
		}
		break;

	default:goto unknown;
	}

	return;

unknown:
	fatal("arm_coproc_i80321_6: unimplemented opcode1=%i opcode2=%i crn="
	    "%i crm=%i rd=%i l=%i)\n", opcode1, opcode2, crn, crm, rd, l_bit);
	exit(1);
}


/*
 *  arm_coproc_xscale_14():
 *
 *  XScale coprocessor 14, Performance Monitoring Unit.
 */
void arm_coproc_xscale_14(struct cpu *cpu, int opcode1, int opcode2, int l_bit,
	int crn, int crm, int rd)
{
	if (opcode2 != 0) {
		fatal("TODO: opcode2 = %i\n", opcode2);
		goto unknown;
	}

	switch (crm) {

	case 0:	switch (crn) {
		case 0:	if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.xsc1_pmnc;
			else
				cpu->cd.arm.xsc1_pmnc = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			break;
		case 1:	if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.xsc1_ccnt;
			else
				cpu->cd.arm.xsc1_ccnt = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			break;
		case 2:	if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.xsc1_pmn0;
			else
				cpu->cd.arm.xsc1_pmn0 = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			break;
		case 3:	if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.xsc1_pmn1;
			else
				cpu->cd.arm.xsc1_pmn1 = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			break;
		case 7:	/*  UNIMPLEMENTED!!! TODO  */
			/*  Possibly some kind of idle or sleep function.  */
			break;
		default:goto unknown;
		}
		break;

	case 1:	switch (crn) {
		case 0:	if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.xsc2_pmnc;
	while (me++ < 63)
		tmp &= ~((uint64_t)1 << (63-me));
	cpu->cd.ppc.gpr[ra] = tmp;
	if (rc)
		update_cr0(cpu, tmp);
}


/*
 *  rldimi:
 *
 *  arg[0] = copy of the instruction word
 */
X(rldimi)
{
	uint32_t iw = ic->arg[0];
	int rs = (iw >> 21) & 31, ra = (iw >> 16) & 31;
	int sh = ((iw >> 11) & 31) | ((iw & 2) << 4);
	int mb = ((iw >> 6) & 31) | (iw & 0x20);
	int rc = ic->arg[0] & 1;
	int m, n = sh;
	uint64_t tmp, s = cpu->cd.ppc.gpr[rs];
	/*  TODO: Fix this, its performance is awful:  */
	while (n-- != 0) {
		int b = (s >> 63) & 1;
		s = (s << 1) | b;
	}
	m = mb; tmp = 0;
	do {
		tmp |= ((uint64_t)1 << (63-m));
		m = (m + 1) & 63;
	} while (m != ((64 - sh) & 63));
	cpu->cd.ppc.gpr[ra] &= ~tmp;
	cpu->cd.ppc.gpr[ra] |= (tmp & s);
	if (rc)
		update_cr0(cpu, cpu->cd.ppc.gpr[ra]);
}


/*
 *  rlwnm:
 *
 *  arg[0] = ptr to ra
 *  arg[1] = mask
 *  arg[2] = copy of the instruction word
 */
X(rlwnm)
{
	uint32_t tmp, iword = ic->arg[2];
	int rs = (iword >> 21) & 31;
	int rb = (iword >> 11) & 31;
/*
 *  rlwimi:
 *
 *  arg[0] = ptr to rs
 *  arg[1] = ptr to ra
 *  arg[2] = copy of the instruction word
 */
X(rlwimi)
{
	uint32_t tmp = (uint32_t)reg(ic->arg[0]);
	MODE_uint_t ra = reg(ic->arg[1]);
	uint32_t iword = ic->arg[2];
	int sh = (iword >> 11) & 31;
	int mb = (iword >> 6) & 31;
	int me = (iword >> 1) & 31;   
	int rc = iword & 1;

	tmp = sh ? ((tmp << sh) | (tmp >> (32-sh))) : tmp;

	/*  A wrapping 64-bit MASK(MB+32, ME+32) selects the DUPLICATED high
	    word (Book I :578 -- MASK wraps when x > y).  Assignment to a
	    32-bit MODE_uint_t retains only the low word, which is a sound
	    representation argument by defined unsigned-conversion semantics
	    and NOT an accident: the shift is performed at 64-bit width and
	    the loop below then does the low-word insertion.

	    IT IS NOT A CLAIM THAT ARCHITECTURAL 32-BIT MODE REMOVES HALF THE
	    REGISTER, and the earlier one-line wording here invited that
	    reading.  Book I S1.2 (:342) says both execution modes affect all
	    64 bits when an instruction sets a 64-bit register and the result
	    is mode-independent, and :1989 describes every GPR as 64 bits --
	    so a 64-bit processor running in 32-bit MODE still owes this
	    write.  Whether any such implementation compiles the narrow
	    variant is NOT established here; if one does, it loses the
	    architectural high word and that is a defect.  Filed rather than
	    guessed.  (This file's idiom is MODE32 with #else -- see extsb /
	    extsh -- and carries no MODE64 conditional, so a future guard
	    would be #ifndef MODE32.)  */
	if (mb > me)
		ra = (ra & 0xffffffffULL) | ((uint64_t)tmp << 32);

	for (;;) {
		uint64_t mask;
		mask = (uint64_t)1 << (31-mb);
		ra &= ~mask;
		ra |= (tmp & mask);
		if (mb == me)
			break;
		mb ++;
		if (mb == 32)
			mb = 0;
	}
	reg(ic->arg[1]) = ra;
	if (rc)
		update_cr0(cpu, ra);
}


/*
 *  srawi:
 *
 *  arg[0] = ptr to rs
 *  arg[1] = ptr to ra
 *  arg[2] = sh (shift amount)
 */
X(srawi)
{
	uint32_t tmp = reg(ic->arg[0]);
	int i = 0, j = 0, sh = ic->arg[2];
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""ppcrldimimask -- RUNG-3 DETECTOR for X(rldimi) mask bounds and wrapping defects.

DEFECT.  src/cpus/cpu_ppc_instr.c, X(rldimi):

    uint32_t iw = ic->arg[0];
    int rs = (iw >> 21) & 31, ra = (iw >> 16) & 31;
    int sh = ((iw >> 11) & 31) | ((iw & 2) << 4);
    int mb = ((iw >> 6) & 31) | (iw & 0x20);
    int rc = ic->arg[0] & 1;
    int m;
    uint64_t tmp, s = cpu->cd.ppc.gpr[rs];
    while (sh-- != 0) { ... }
    m = mb; tmp = 0;
    do {

THE DEFECT HAS TWO DIRECTIONS:
  (1) The rotate loop `while (sh-- != 0)` post-decrements, leaving `sh == -1` on exit
      for every SH (including SH=0).
  (2) The termination test `m != 63 - sh` is therefore evaluated with `sh == -1`,
      becoming `m != 64` always. The mask generated is always MB..63 instead of MB..63-SH.

  Consequences on two classes of encodings:
  - Non-wrapping (MB <= 63-SH): the mask fails to stop at 63-SH and runs all the way
    to 63, OVERWRITING the low SH bits of destination RA that must be preserved.
  - Wrapping (MB > 63-SH): the mask stops at bit 63 and OMITS the required insertion
    into positions 0..63-SH, leaving those destination bits unchanged.

THE FOUR-EDIT REPAIR:
  1. Save SH in a separate variable: `int m, n = sh;`
  2. Consume the copy in the rotate loop: `while (n-- != 0)`
  3. Circular increment in the mask loop: `m = (m + 1) & 63;`
  4. Compare against successor modulo 64: `while (m != ((64 - sh) & 63));`

A partial repair saving SH but using non-wrapping `m++` and `m != 64 - sh` hits
`(uint64_t)1 << -1` at MB=40, SH=40 (undefined behaviour in C).

ARCHITECTURE, PowerPC Book I v2.01:4315-4327 (rldimi RTL and prose):
  n  <- sh[5] || sh[0:4]
  r  <- ROTL64((RS), n)
  b  <- mb[5] || mb[0:4]
  m  <- MASK(b, ~n)               where ~n on 6-bit field is 63-SH
  RA <- (r & m) | ((RA) & ~m)

  MASK(x, y) is defined at Book I:578 as "1s in positions x through y (wrapping if x > y)".
  PEM:6882 and Book I:4031 confirm "It is not possible to specify an all-zero mask."

CLAUSE (e) DISCLOSURE (what this instrument cannot see):
  (1) Model width vs mode width: the probe runs on `-E bareppc -M 64` (PPC970, 64-bit
      model, MSR[SF]=1). It cannot observe a 64-bit CPU operating in 32-bit mode (MSR[SF]=0),
      where `update_cr0` branching on `cpu->cd.ppc.bits == 64` would compare 64 bits instead
      of 32 bits (filed separately as ppccr0modewidth).
  (2) Unimplemented instructions: `rldic` (XO=2), `rldcl` (XO=8), `rldcr` (XO=9) are not
      implemented in the tree's decoder (`goto bad`) and cannot be driven here.
  (3) Full OS boot rigs: no 64-bit PowerPC guest OS boot image exists in the tree; rung 3
      unmodified machine guest execution is the highest achievable rung.

SHIPPED CONTRACT: 29 rows; 495 distinct census cells.  The census uses an all-ones source and
does not test the rotate amount independently of the mask bounds -- the pattern rows do, at
exactly three rotate amounts (SH 8, 24, 40: `pres nowrap', `full rot', `pres wrap'), so an edit
keyed on one other SH value is NOT pinned.  `full rot' is the one full-width mask from a patterned
source; `alias rs=ra' is the one rA == rS row.  The CR row checks readback PRESENCE only; CR0 value
semantics are not covered.  No rlwimi rows here.

Sentinel: PPC_RLDIMI_PROBE_DONE (plus PPC_RLDIMI_RESULT=n/N, PPC_RLDIMI_CONTROL=OK|FAIL)
"""

import os
import re
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile
import time

if len(sys.argv) < 2:
    sys.stderr.write("usage: ppc_rldimi_probe.py <gxemul> [label]\n")
    sys.exit(2)

#  The loader needs a 4-byte stub; it is private to this run (the witnesses shared one /tmp path).
