/*
 *  arm_coproc_i80321_6():
 *
 *  Intel 80321 coprocessor 6.
 */
void arm_coproc_i80321_6(struct cpu *cpu, int opcode1, int opcode2, int l_bit,
	int crn, int crm, int rd)
{
	switch (crm) {

	case 0:	switch (crn) {
		case 0:	if (l_bit) {
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.i80321_inten;
			} else
				cpu->cd.arm.i80321_inten = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			break;
		case 4:	if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.i80321_isteer;
			else {
				cpu->cd.arm.i80321_isteer = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
				if (cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] != 0) {
					fatal("ARM xscale interrupt steering"
					    " is not yet implemented\n");
					exit(1);
				}
			}
			break;
		case 8:	if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.i80321_isrc;
			else {
				cpu->cd.arm.i80321_isrc = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
				fatal("TODO: XScale int ack?\n");
				exit(1);
			}
			break;
		default:goto unknown;
		}
		break;

	case 1:	/*  fatal("TIMER opcode1=%i opcode2=%i crn="
    "%i crm=%i rd=%i l=%i)\n", opcode1, opcode2, crn, crm, rd, l_bit);  */

		switch (crn) {
		case 0:	/*  tmr0:  */
			if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.tmr0;
			else
				cpu->cd.arm.tmr0 = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			break;
		case 1:	/*  tmr1:  */
			if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.tmr1;
			else
				cpu->cd.arm.tmr1 = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			break;
		case 2:	/*  tcr0:  */
			if (l_bit) {
				/*  NOTE/TODO: Ugly hack: timer increment  */
				cpu->cd.arm.tcr0 ++;
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.tcr0;
			} else {
				cpu->cd.arm.tcr0 = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			}
			break;
		case 3:	/*  tcr1:  */
			if (l_bit) {
				/*  NOTE/TODO: Ugly hack: timer increment  */
				cpu->cd.arm.tcr1 ++;
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.tcr1;
			} else {
				cpu->cd.arm.tcr1 = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			}
			break;
		case 4:	/*  trr0:  */
			if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.trr0;
			else
				cpu->cd.arm.trr0 = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			break;
		case 5:	/*  trr1:  */
			if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.trr1;
			else
				cpu->cd.arm.trr1 = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			break;
		case 6:	/*  tisr:  */
			if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.tisr;
			else {
				/*  Writing clears interrupts:  */
				cpu->cd.arm.tisr &= ~cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];

				if (!(cpu->cd.arm.tisr & TISR_TMR0))
					INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(
					    cpu->cd.arm.tmr0_irq);
				if (!(cpu->cd.arm.tisr & TISR_TMR1))
					INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(
					    cpu->cd.arm.tmr1_irq);
			}
			break;
		case 7:	/*  wdtcr:  */
			if (l_bit)
				cpu->cd.arm.r[rd] = cpu->cd.arm.wdtcr;
			else
				cpu->cd.arm.wdtcr = cpu->cd.arm.r[rd];
			break;
		default:goto unknown;
		}

# Session 3: Destination preservation with structured data and seeded rA
#  Two rows added on pass 2 (2026-09-12), both from a PATTERNED source:
#   * `full rot': every full-width mask elsewhere (`wrap full', and the census's six full-mask cells)
#     uses the all-ones source, so an edit skipping the ROTATE only when the mask is full --
#     `while (n-- != 0 && mb != ((64 - sh) & 63))', codex -- scored green.  SH=24, MB=40 is full
#     (MB == 64-SH) and also puts a third rotate amount (24) beside `pres nowrap' (8) and `pres wrap' (40).
#   * `alias rs=ra': rlwimi carries three aliasing rows and rldimi carried none, so a merge that
#     re-reads gpr[rs] AFTER clearing gpr[ra] -- instead of the snapshot `s' -- was green (glm).
#     r8 is both source and destination, seeded with RS_VAL; SH=8, MB=16 is a partial mask.
s3_setup = seed([(SRC, RS_VAL)] + [(r, SENT) for r in DESTS] + [(8, RS_VAL)])
s3_insns = [
    RLDIMI(4, SRC, 8, 0),        # nowrap: preserve low 8 bits of rA (bits 56..63)
    RLDIMI(5, SRC, 40, 40),      # wrap: preserve unmasked bits 24..39 of rA
    RLDIMI(6, SRC, 0, 32),       # nowrap: preserve high 32 bits (bits 0..31)
    RLDIMI(7, SRC, 24, 40),      # full mask from a patterned source: the rotate must show
    RLDIMI(8, 8, 8, 16),         # rA == rS: the merge must use the value read before the write
]
s3_regs, s3_dis, s3_T, s3_how = session(s3_setup, s3_insns)
want_s3_dis = ["rldimi", "rldimi", "rldimi", "rldimi", "rldimi"]
want_s3_ops = ["r4,r3,8,0", "r5,r3,40,40", "r6,r3,0,32", "r7,r3,24,40", "r8,r8,8,16"]
disasm_checked += len(want_s3_dis)
disasm_ok += sum(1 for i, (_, m, o) in enumerate(s3_dis[:len(want_s3_dis)])
                 if m == want_s3_dis[i] and o == want_s3_ops[i])

row("pres nowrap", hex(s3_regs.get(4, -1)), hex(want_rldimi(RS_VAL, SENT, 8, 0)),
    "preserve unmasked destination bits 56..63")
row("pres wrap", hex(s3_regs.get(5, -1)), hex(want_rldimi(RS_VAL, SENT, 40, 40)),
    "preserve unmasked destination bits 24..39")
row("pres sh0", hex(s3_regs.get(6, -1)), hex(want_rldimi(RS_VAL, SENT, 0, 32)),
    "preserve unmasked high 32 bits 0..31")
row("full rot", hex(s3_regs.get(7, -1)), hex(want_rldimi(RS_VAL, SENT, 24, 40)),
    "full mask, patterned source: result is ROTL64(rS,24)")
row("alias rs=ra", hex(s3_regs.get(8, -1)), hex(want_rldimi(RS_VAL, RS_VAL, 8, 16)),
    "rA == rS: merge uses rS as read, not the cleared rA")

# ========================================================= CR0 RECORD FORM =====
# rldimi. with Rc=1
cr_setup = seed([(SRC, RS_VAL), (4, SENT)])
cr_insns = [
    RLDIMI(4, SRC, 8, 0, rc=1),
]
cr_regs, cr_dis, cr_T, cr_how = session(cr_setup, cr_insns)
row("cr0 record form", "exit0" if "cr" in cr_regs else "no-cr", "exit0",
    "rldimi. executes with Rc=1 and dumps CR")

# ==================================================== COMPREHENSIVE CENSUS =====
# Sweep structured combinations across the full (SH, MB) space:
# - MB sweeps at fixed SH in {0, 8, 40, 63} (4 x 64 = 256)
# - SH sweeps at fixed MB in {0, 40} (2 x 64 = 128)
# - Diagonal (k, k) for k in 0..63 (64)
# - Anti-diagonal (k, 63-k) for k in 0..63 (64)
census_combos = []
for sh_val in [0, 8, 40, 63]:
    for mb_val in range(64):
        census_combos.append((sh_val, mb_val))
for mb_val in [0, 40]:
    for sh_val in range(64):
        census_combos.append((sh_val, mb_val))
for k in range(64):
    census_combos.append((k, k))
    census_combos.append((k, (63 - k) & 63))

seen = set()
uniq_census = []
for c in census_combos:
    if c not in seen:
        seen.add(c)
        uniq_census.append(c)

census_fail = 0
census_zero = 0
census_run = 0

for base in range(0, len(uniq_census), len(DESTS)):
    chunk = uniq_census[base:base + len(DESTS)]
    insns = [RLDIMI(DESTS[i], SRC, sh, mb) for i, (sh, mb) in enumerate(chunk)]
    st = seed([(SRC, RS_ONES)] + [(r, 0) for r in DESTS])
    cregs, cdis, cT, chow = session(st, insns)
    if chow != "exit0":
        print("CENSUS SESSION ABORTED how=%s at base=%d" % (chow, base))
        break
    for i, (sh, mb) in enumerate(chunk):
        disasm_checked += 1
        if i < len(cdis) and cdis[i][1] == "rldimi" and \
           cdis[i][2] == "r%d,r%d,%d,%d" % (DESTS[i], SRC, sh, mb):
            disasm_ok += 1
    for i, (sh, mb) in enumerate(chunk):
        census_run += 1
        got = cregs.get(DESTS[i], None)
        exp = want_rldimi(RS_ONES, 0, sh, mb)
        if got != exp:
            census_fail += 1
        if got == 0:
            census_zero += 1

#  Both wants are LITERALS (measured: killtable_20260911/probe_BASE.raw, 511/511 and 495).  They were
#  `len(uniq_census)' and `disasm_checked' -- derived from the very lists they count, so a census
#  shrunk by an edit passed both rows (codex, promotion design 2026-09-12).  511 = 16 control/core + 495;
#  the two pass-2 rows (`full rot', `alias rs=ra') add two placed words, so 513 -- re-measured in
#  ppcrlwimiwidth/killtable3_20260912/ (BASE leg), not carried.
row("disasm total", "%d/%d" % (disasm_ok, disasm_checked), "513/513",
    "every placed word re-read by disassembler")
row("census run", census_run, 495,
    "(SH,MB) cells driven across sweeps and diagonals")
row("census wrong", census_fail, 0,
    "cells differing from architectural result (0 after fix)")
row("census zero", census_zero, 0,
    "cells returning zero (0 after fix)")

# ================================================================ SCORE + DONE
npass = sum(1 for _, ok in rows if ok)
print("")
print("SCORE %d/%d  elapsed=%.1fs  binary=%s  label=%s"
      % (npass, len(rows), time.time() - t0, BIN, LABEL))
print("FAILING: " + ", ".join(n for n, ok in rows if not ok))
#  CONTROL = rows green on EVERY build, fixed or not.  Checked against every recorded leg
#  (killtable_20260911/, killtable_nullmedium_20260911/): green on HEAD and on every mutant but

# ========================================================= CR0 RECORD FORM =====
# rlwimi. with Rc=1
cr_setup = seed([(SRC, RS_VAL), (4, SENT)])
cr_insns = [
    RLWIMI(4, SRC, 8, 0, 15, rc=1),
]

row("cr0 record form", "exit0" if "cr" in cr_regs else "no-cr", "exit0",
    "rlwimi. executes with Rc=1 and dumps CR")

# ==================================================== COMPREHENSIVE CENSUS =====
# Sweep structured combinations across (SH, MB, ME) space:
# - Quadrant 1: SH=0, MB<=ME (non-wrapping, zero shift)
# - Quadrant 2: SH in {1, 8, 16, 31}, MB<=ME (non-wrapping, shift > 0: width contamination)
# - Quadrant 3: SH=0, MB>ME (wrapping, zero shift: missing high-half)
# - Quadrant 4: SH in {1, 8, 16, 31}, MB>ME (wrapping, shift > 0: both defects)
census_combos = []
# Fixed representative (MB, ME) pairs:
mb_me_nowrap = [(0, 31), (0, 15), (8, 23), (16, 31), (10, 20), (0, 0), (31, 31)]
mb_me_wrap = [(31, 0), (24, 8), (20, 10), (28, 4), (1, 0), (31, 30), (16, 8)]

for sh_val in [0, 1, 8, 16, 31]:
    for mb, me in mb_me_nowrap:
        census_combos.append((sh_val, mb, me))
    for mb, me in mb_me_wrap:
        census_combos.append((sh_val, mb, me))

# Add diagonal and boundary sweeps:
for k in range(32):
    census_combos.append((k, k, (31 - k)))
    census_combos.append((k, (31 - k), k))

seen = set()
uniq_census = []
for c in census_combos:
    if c not in seen:
        seen.add(c)
        uniq_census.append(c)

census_fail = 0
census_zero = 0
census_run = 0

for base in range(0, len(uniq_census), len(DESTS)):
    chunk = uniq_census[base:base + len(DESTS)]
    insns = [RLWIMI(DESTS[i], SRC, sh, mb, me) for i, (sh, mb, me) in enumerate(chunk)]
    st = seed([(SRC, RS_VAL)] + [(r, SENT) for r in DESTS])
    cregs, cdis, cT, chow = session(st, insns)
    if chow != "exit0":
        print("CENSUS SESSION ABORTED how=%s at base=%d" % (chow, base))
        break
    for i, (sh, mb, me) in enumerate(chunk):
        disasm_checked += 1
        if i < len(cdis) and cdis[i][1] == "rlwimi" and \
           cdis[i][2] == "r%d,r%d,%d,%d,%d" % (DESTS[i], SRC, sh, mb, me):
            disasm_ok += 1
    for i, (sh, mb, me) in enumerate(chunk):
        census_run += 1
        got = cregs.get(DESTS[i], None)
        exp = want_rlwimi(RS_VAL, SENT, sh, mb, me)
        if got != exp:
            census_fail += 1
        if got == 0:
            census_zero += 1

#  Both wants are LITERALS (measured: killtable_20260911/probe_BASE.raw, 146/146 and 129).  They were
#  `len(uniq_census)' and `disasm_checked' -- derived from the very lists they count, so a census
#  shrunk by an edit passed both rows (codex, promotion design 2026-09-12).  146 = 3+4+4+3+3+129.
row("disasm total", "%d/%d" % (disasm_ok, disasm_checked), "146/146",
    "every placed word re-read by disassembler")
row("census run", census_run, 129,
    "(SH,MB,ME) cells driven across sweeps and diagonals")
row("census wrong", census_fail, 0,
    "cells differing from architectural result (0 after fix)")
row("census zero", census_zero, 0,
    "cells returning zero (0 after fix)")

print("SCORE %d/%d  elapsed=%.1fs  binary=%s  label=%s"

>
> **THE OBLIGATION FOLLOWS THE MATERIAL, NOT THE NARRATIVE.** A round that repairs a detector
> in response to a panel has produced NEW floor-bound material, and "the round already had its
> pass 2" is a fact about the narrative. `#499` ran four passes and **the first three each
> found a real defect, two of them MEASURED CLEAN SWEEPS of a detector that had just come back
> green.**
>
> **THE STOP CONDITION: one pass per floor-bound diff, closing on the FIRST PASS THAT FINDS
> ZERO BREACHES** -- subject to the two corrections below, without which it closes early on a
> live escape.
>
> #### CORRECTION 1 -- A ZERO NEEDS A VALIDITY CONDITION ON THE PASS, AND A DENOMINATOR
>
> As first written, "zero breaches" counted a pass's OUTPUT with no condition on the pass
> itself. **Three briefs that day went out with the diff missing** -- a `git show | head -N`
> inlined the commit message instead -- and a panel answering "no breaches" against an empty
> packet would have CLOSED THE CHAIN.
>
> > **A closing pass must be a VALID pass: the diff actually delivered; the roster complete or
> > its misses named per the seat ledger; and the zero carried WITH ITS DENOMINATOR -- which
> > cells, which mutant shapes, were swept.** *"Zero found"* and *"zero looked for"* must not
> > render the same, which is this project's oldest rule about blanks turned on its own stop
> > condition.
>
> That also converts the rule's finishing incentive into a checkable artifact: **a bare zero
> does not close; a zero with an enumeration does.**
>
> #### CORRECTION 2 -- SCOPE: EFFORT ON THE DIFF, THE FLOOR QUESTION ON THE COMPOSITE
>
> *"Scoped to that diff"* is ambiguous and **the dangerous reading would have closed `#499`
> over a live total escape.** The breach that pass 3 found was not in that pass's two hunks --
> the hunks were fine. It lived in the SHIPPED COMPOSITE detector's coverage of the ORIGINAL
> fix's mutant space.
>
> > **Review EFFORT is scoped to the repair diff, so settled design is not re-litigated. The
> > FLOOR QUESTION -- does a clean-sweep mutant exist in the (d) enumeration -- is asked of the
> > SHIPPED COMPOSITE on every pass.**
>
> #### THE BOUND IS IMPORTED, NOT INTRINSIC -- state the dependency or the rule overclaims
>
> The stop condition does not bound the chain and must not pretend to. The bound comes from
> **the (d) DEPENDS-ON enumeration being diff-closed and finite**, and from *zero breaches*
> meaning **zero SURVIVING ADJUDICATION** rather than zero raised -- otherwise one stubborn
> seat blocks closure forever, against the count-arguments-not-seats rule. With those imports
> the chain is infinite only if every repair is defective, in which case every pass is buying a
> real defect off the ship.
>
> #### *** THE AMENDMENT THAT ACTUALLY ENDS CHAINS, and it is the part worth carrying. ***
>
> `#499`'s three breaches were **THE SAME CLASS THREE TIMES** -- a cell of one state space left
> empty, repaired at exactly the address the mutant had used, leaving the symmetric cell open

import ast
from pathlib import Path
M = (1 << 64) - 1
S = 0xDEADBEEFCAFEF00D
A = 0x5A5A5A5A5A5A5A5A
def rot(x, n):
    return ((x << n) | (x >> (64 - n))) & M if n else x & M
def mask(b, e):
    low = (1 << (64-b)) - 1
    high = M "'^ ((1 << (63-e)) - 1)
    return low & high if b <= e else low | high
def good(rs, ra, sh, mb):
    m = mask(mb, 63-sh)
    return (ra & (M ''^ m)) | (rot(rs,sh) & m)
def late(rs, ra, sh, mb, alias):
    m = mask(mb,63-sh)
    cleared = ra & (M ''^ m)
    return cleared | (rot(cleared if alias else rs,sh) & m)
for sh,mb in [(8,16),(40,40),(24,40),(0,32)]:
    g = good(S,S,sh,mb)
    l = late(S,S,sh,mb,True)
    print(f'"'ALIAS sh={sh} mb={mb}: mask={mask(mb,63-sh):#018x}, good={g:#018x}, late={l:#018x}, wrap_only_late={(l if mb > 63-sh else g):#018x}')
print(f'Rc=1 rldimi fixture: correct={good(S,A,8,0):#018x}, skipped={A:#018x}')
r32 = rot((S & 0xffffffff) * 0x100000001,8)
m32 = mask(32,47)
print(f'Rc=1 rlwimi fixture: correct={((A & (M "'^ m32)) | (r32 & m32)):#018x}, skipped={A:#018x}'"')
print(f'SH=0 pres sh0: correct={good(S,A,0,32):#018x}, wrongly_rotate_one={((A & 0xffffffff00000000) | (rot(S,1) & 0xffffffff)):#018x}')
print('Mask-loop cross-check: ',end='')
for sh in range(64):
    for mb in range(64):
        idx=mb; actual=0; count=0
        while True:
            actual |= 1 << (63-idx)
            idx=(idx+1)&63
            count+=1
            if idx==((64-sh)&63): break
            assert count < 64
        assert actual==mask(mb,63-sh)
print('all 4096 SH/MB masks equal closed-form reference, each terminates in 1..64 steps')
for name in ['rldimi','rlwimi']:
    p=Path(f'regress/ppc_{name}_probe.py')

# believed -- this harness has been bitten by a probe that reported a defect off

# The rounding-mode WRITE channel is proven once per mode, in its own sessions, by
# reading FPSCR back through the guest's own mffs. Both numbers are asserted, because
# "0 bad" is also what a probe reports when it never read FPSCR at all: the first
# version parsed the debugger's plain `reg`, which does not print FPSCR on this CPU,
# so the check passed vacuously on every run until a diff-review seat predicted the
# hole and the rewrite measured it (43 rows of "unparsed").

# The table must keep enough of each class to be worth running: a gate whose rows are
# all pins cannot detect a reverted fix, and one with no pins cannot detect collateral
# damage. Both counts are asserted rather than trusted.

# Both floors are set to the ACTUAL counts, not below them. They had drifted to 47/26
# against real counts of 50/36, which means a whole class could have lost rows without
# the floor noticing -- the same "a check that cannot fail" species this gate's header
# warns about twice. The two numbers must also sum to the asserted `rows run` above
# (94 + 44 = 138); if they stop summing, a row lost its class rather than the table
# losing a row.

138-* **S_ISRC_WR**: an isrc write still dies.  **SIB_READ**: crn-4 read survives.  **SIB_WRITE**: crn-4
139:  non-zero write still dies.
140-* Fresh sessions: **D_OP1** (read with **opcode1=1**, want S1); **INTEN_WRITE_PRESERVES_ISTEER**
141-  (isteer<-0, inten<-S1, read isteer, want 0); **D_CRM1_TMR0** (tmr0<-0x5A5A0000, inten<-S1, read
142-  crm=1/crn=0, want 0x5A5A0000).
143-* **RD15_SURVIVE**: an inten read into r15 survives to the marker.
144:* **ANCHOR** (free-running): inten<-bit 28; the UART asserts irq 28 (`ANCHOR_ISRC`); read inten while
145-  isrc != 0 (`ANCHOR_M1`); unmask CPSR.I and spin — the IRQ must reach vector 0x18 (`ANCHOR_EXC`);
146-  contrast: inten = 0 spins and 0x18 is never reached (`ANCHOR_CONTRAST`).
147-* `A2_INSTALLED` (CP6 installed).
--

| F1 | **UPHOLD** | The escape and its closure are measured; that establishes discrimination, independently of its floor classification. |
| F2 | **REFINE** | All-ones kills every predicate “exit when this single inten bit is set”; it does not establish arbitrary bit-pattern coverage. |
| F3 | **UPHOLD** | M5 redirects both reads and writes; the corrected description matches the mutator. |
| F4 | **UPHOLD your rejection** | The two-way mutation explains both failures. Mutator text plus the existing observations suffice; another measurement is unnecessary. |
| F5 | **UPHOLD** | Equivalent within the reachable sessions of this single mutant: the nonzero isteer setup already terminates the host. |
| F6 | **UPHOLD** | ACCEPT fits an already implemented register read; the Footbridge precedent supports disposition, not quiet source behavior. |
| F7 | **UPHOLD** | Zero-valued substitutes defeat the named value discrimination; host death still legitimately fails the anchor’s required behavior. |
| F8 | **UPHOLD** | The read-count ceiling and single IRQ source are relevant, bounded disclosures. |
| F9 | **UPHOLD** | The patterned full-mask row independently exposes the skipped rotation, as measured. |
| F10 | **REFINE** | The normalized mutant tests aliasing correctly. Neither its kill nor one alias row establishes general alias coverage; also, unrotated input can agree at nonzero SH for invariant sources. |
| F11 | **REFINE** | The two assertions demonstrably fail, but the measurement does **not** isolate the benefit of making their expectations literal. |
| F12 | **REFINE** | The exclusive list is wrong: `pres sh0` supplies patterned **SH=0** coverage. The list is 0, 8, 24, 40—or three **nonzero** amounts. |
| F13 | **UPHOLD** | The wrapping low-word mask selects all 32 low bits. |
| F14 | **REFINE** | The missing disclosure is owed; additionally, neither record-form row checks the destination-register result. |
| F15 | **REFINE** | Host shift-count masking does not establish C equivalence through undefined behavior. Retaining the guard is correct; no additional detectable failure has been established. |
| F16 | **UPHOLD** | An isolated, otherwise untested SH case is disclosure under the packet’s stated bound. |
| D1 | **OVERTURN the provisional rule** | Under the packet’s body-tested-case bound, both F1 and F2 are disclosure; test cost cannot create a floor partition. |
| D2 | **UPHOLD** | The actual mutator settles the disagreement in favor of the two-way explanation. |
| D3 | **REFINE** | No floor breach follows merely from lacking a separate measurement of the literal expectations’ contribution; that evidentiary limitation should be recorded. |

On D1, I withdraw the earlier Codex argument for `rd == 0` **under your stated bound**. Reading an index establishes a dependency, not a tested zero/nonzero partition. Importing another operand’s `== 0` must not bypass the body-tested-case restriction. For the next deletion, enumerate the permitted guards and swaps, then apply that bound; do not invent cases merely because the body reads their operands.

Nor is the alternative “all registers are unbounded”: sixteen registers are plainly finite. Raw value-space finiteness simply is not your enumeration rule. Both additions are **L1 construction regardless**; the local [L1/L2 definitions](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/tools/CLAUDE.md:2397) distinguish unshipped construction from a shipped detector’s breach.

The accepted statements that are wrong as written are F12’s exclusive shift list and F15’s equivalence rationale. F11 also becomes wrong if “these rows failed” is treated as “literalizing them caused the detection.” I would retain every added row. The acceptance count provides no reason to manufacture a rejection.

For F11, the counterfactual is decisive:

- `census run = 0` also fails the old derived expectation `len(uniq_census) = 495`.
- `disasm total = 8/16` also fails the old derived expectation `16/16`.
- Other rows already reject that mutant.

Thus the observation proves assertion failures, not the literals’ additional protection. If you want that protection **measured**, name the experiment `CENSUS_SHRINK`: shorten the detector’s census on a correct emulator and compare derived versus literal expectations.

Three further gaps emerged. These are **static predictions supported by arithmetic checks, not new emulator measurements**:

1. **The patterned shifts all preserve a byte boundary.** Changing only the rotation copy to `n = sh & ~7` is predicted to survive **29/29**: patterned shifts are 0, 8, 24, 40, and every other source is all-ones. This is broader than the isolated SH=1/2 examples. A patterned full-mask case SH=63, MB=1 would distinguish it: correct `0xef56df77e57f7806`, rounded-rotation result `0x0ddeadbeefcafef0`. Name the measurement `D_ROT_BYTE`.

2. **Yes, an aliasing mutant survives F10’s new row.** Use the late, rotated reread only for wrapping masks, retaining the snapshot otherwise. Nonaliasing executions remain correct; your sole alias row is nonwrapping. Predicted result: **29/29**. Name it `D_ALIAS_LATE_WRAP`; an alias case SH=40, MB=40 with RS_VAL should produce `0xfef00defcabeefca`, while that late reread produces `0x000000efca00efca`. This does not justify demanding every alias combination as a floor cell.

3. **Record-form blindness includes the instruction’s data result.** A computation skipped only for `Rc=1` is predicted to survive both probes. Their [rldimi](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/ppc_rldimi_probe.py:384) and [rlwimi](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/ppc_rlwimi_probe.py:405) rows merely find a CR register in the dump. Name the measurements `D_RC_NO_RESULT` and `R_RC_NO_RESULT`. Either check the destination in those sessions or explicitly disclose this additional limitation; “CR0 values untested” alone understates it.

I found no source-fix error in the inspected changes. A separate arithmetic check matched all **4,096 rldimi SH/MB masks**, with termination within 64 iterations. The three current probes also matched their table-3 frozen copies byte for byte.

**XScale can close after its 30-row gate is green and the grading record is corrected.** No additional rd enumeration is owed by this adjudication.

**PPC is not ready in the inspected tree.** Neither new probe is referenced by the gate scripts or `regress/run.sh`; a green existing PPC gate therefore does not validate this delivery. Wire them into an executed gate, finish F14’s disclosure, correct F12, and update the probes’ stale “controls green on every build” comments—the recorded mutant legs contradict those comments. Review the resulting gate/detector changes before closure. The additional disclosure-class examples above need not force another expansion.

No gates, battery, or emulator mutants were run.

Their [rldimi](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/ppc_rldimi_probe.py:384) and [rlwimi](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/ppc_rlwimi_probe.py:405) rows merely find a CR register in the dump. Name the measurements `D_RC_NO_RESULT` and `R_RC_NO_RESULT`. Either check the destination in those sessions or explicitly disclose this additional limitation; “CR0 values untested” alone understates it.

I found no source-fix error in the inspected changes. A separate arithmetic check matched all **4,096 rldimi SH/MB masks**, with termination within 64 iterations. The three current probes also matched their table-3 frozen copies byte for byte.

**XScale can close after its 30-row gate is green and the grading record is corrected.** No additional rd enumeration is owed by this adjudication.
